## Purpose
This file contains a packed representation of the entire repository's contents.
It is designed to be easily consumable by AI systems for analysis, code review,
or other automated processes.

## File Format
The content is organized as follows:
1. This summary section

3. Directory structure
4. Multiple file entries, each consisting of:
  a. A header with the file path (## File: path/to/file)
  b. The full contents of the file in a code block

## Usage Guidelines
- This file should be treated as read-only. Any changes should be made to the
  original repository files, not this packed version.
- When processing this file, use the file path to distinguish
  between different files in the repository.
- Be aware that this file may contain sensitive information. Handle it with
  the same level of security as you would the original repository.

  configuration.
- Binary files are not included in this packed representation. Please refer to
  the Repository Structure section for a complete list of file paths, including
  binary files.

```markdown
* * *

name: Bug report
about: Create a report to help us improve
title: ''
labels: ''
assignees: ''

* * *

**Describe the bug**
A clear and concise description of what the bug is.

**To Reproduce**
Steps to reproduce the behavior:
1. Go to '...'
2. Click on '....'
3. Scroll down to '....'
4. See error

**Expected behavior**
A clear and concise description of what you expected to happen.

**Screenshots**
If applicable, add screenshots to help explain your problem.

**Desktop (please complete the following information):**

-   OS: [e.g. iOS]
-   Browser [e.g. chrome, safari]
-   Version [e.g. 22]

**Smartphone (please complete the following information):**

-   Device: [e.g. iPhone6]
-   OS: [e.g. iOS8.1]
-   Browser [e.g. stock browser, safari]
-   Version [e.g. 22]

**Additional context**
Add any other context about the problem here.
```

## File: .github/workflows/codeql.yml
```yaml
name: "CodeQL"
on:
  push:
    branches: [ "master" ]
  pull_request:
    branches: [ "master" ]
  schedule:
    - cron: "5 12 * * 2"
jobs:
  analyze:
    name: Analyze
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      actions: read
      contents: read
      security-events: write
    strategy:
      fail-fast: false
      matrix:
        language: [ javascript ]
    steps:
      - name: Checkout
        uses: actions/checkout@v7
      - name: Initialize CodeQL
        uses: github/codeql-action/init@v4
        with:
          languages: ${{ matrix.language }}
          queries: +security-and-quality
      - name: Autobuild
        uses: github/codeql-action/autobuild@v4
      - name: Perform CodeQL Analysis
        uses: github/codeql-action/analyze@v4
        with:
          category: "/language:${{ matrix.language }}"
```

## File: .github/workflows/lighthouse.yml
```yaml
name: Lighthouse CI
on: [push, pull_request]
jobs:
  lighthouse:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
      - name: Setup Node.js
        uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with:
          node-version: '24'
      - name: Setup pnpm
        uses: pnpm/action-setup@0977fd99725f1db4007ccb2928dbb4e90d06cc86 # v6
      - name: Get pnpm store directory
        shell: bash
        run: |
          echo "STORE_PATH=$(pnpm store path --silent)" >> $GITHUB_ENV
      - name: Setup pnpm cache
        uses: actions/cache@v6
        with:
          path: ${{ env.STORE_PATH }}
          key: ${{ runner.os }}-pnpm-store-${{ hashFiles('**/pnpm-lock.yaml') }}
          restore-keys: |
            ${{ runner.os }}-pnpm-store-
      - name: Install dependencies
        run: pnpm install --ignore-scripts
      - name: Build project
        env:
          NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL: ${{ secrets.NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL }}
          NEXT_PUBLIC_PLACEHOLDER_SMALL_IMAGE_URL: "https://via.placeholder.com/200"
          NEXT_PUBLIC_PLACEHOLDER_LARGE_IMAGE_URL: "https://via.placeholder.com/600"
        run: pnpm build
      - name: Start server
        env:
          NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL: ${{ secrets.NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL }}
          NEXT_PUBLIC_PLACEHOLDER_SMALL_IMAGE_URL: "https://via.placeholder.com/200"
          NEXT_PUBLIC_PLACEHOLDER_LARGE_IMAGE_URL: "https://via.placeholder.com/600"
        run: |
          pnpm start &
          echo "Waiting for server to be ready..."
          while ! nc -z localhost 3000; do
            sleep 5
          done
          # Additional wait to ensure full initialization
          sleep 20
      - name: Run Lighthouse CI
        run: |
          pnpm lhci:perf
          pnpm lhci:desktop
        env:
          LHCI_GITHUB_APP_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
      - name: Stop server
        if: always()
        run: |
          pkill -f "next start" || true
```

## File: .github/workflows/pa11y.yml
```yaml
name: A11y
on:
  push:
    branches: [main, master]
  pull_request:
    branches: [main, master]
permissions:
  contents: read
jobs:
  pa11y:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 30
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7
      - name: Setup Node.js
        uses: actions/setup-node@949feb2413d6458794dcd2491c4babbbce0c15c1 # v7
        with:
          node-version: "24"
      - name: Install pnpm
        uses: pnpm/action-setup@2e223e0f0d2b8fd9872cbadb8b7428e5f8b5556d
      - name: Get pnpm store directory
        shell: bash
        run: |
          echo "STORE_PATH=$(pnpm store path --silent)" >> $GITHUB_ENV
      - name: Setup pnpm cache
        uses: actions/cache@55cc8345863c7cc4c66a329aec7e433d2d1c52a9 # v6
        with:
          path: ${{ env.STORE_PATH }}
          key: ${{ runner.os }}-pnpm-store-${{ hashFiles('**/pnpm-lock.yaml') }}
      - name: Install dependencies
        run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts
      - name: Install Chromium for pa11y
        run: pnpm rebuild puppeteer
      - name: Create pa11y config
        run: |
          # Create pa11y config file
          echo '{"chromeLaunchConfig":{"args":["--no-sandbox"]}}' > .pa11y.json
      - name: Build Next.js app
        env:
          NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL: ${{ secrets.NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL }}
          NEXT_PUBLIC_PLACEHOLDER_SMALL_IMAGE_URL: "https://via.placeholder.com/200"
          NEXT_PUBLIC_PLACEHOLDER_LARGE_IMAGE_URL: "https://via.placeholder.com/600"
        run: pnpm build
      - name: Start Next.js app
        env:
          NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL: ${{ secrets.NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL }}
          NEXT_PUBLIC_PLACEHOLDER_SMALL_IMAGE_URL: "https://via.placeholder.com/200"
          NEXT_PUBLIC_PLACEHOLDER_LARGE_IMAGE_URL: "https://via.placeholder.com/600"
        run: pnpm start &
      - name: Wait for the application to be ready
        run: pnpm dlx wait-on http://localhost:3000
      - name: Run pa11y
        run: |
          # Create a directory for the reports
          mkdir -p pa11y-reports
          # Run pa11y on main pages with config
          pnpm exec pa11y --config .pa11y.json http://localhost:3000 > pa11y-reports/home.txt || true
          pnpm exec pa11y --config .pa11y.json http://localhost:3000/produkter > pa11y-reports/produkter.txt || true
          pnpm exec pa11y --config .pa11y.json http://localhost:3000/kategorier > pa11y-reports/kategorier.txt || true
          pnpm exec pa11y --config .pa11y.json http://localhost:3000/handlekurv > pa11y-reports/handlekurv.txt || true
          pnpm exec pa11y --config .pa11y.json http://localhost:3000/kasse > pa11y-reports/kasse.txt || true
          pnpm exec pa11y --config .pa11y.json http://localhost:3000/logg-inn > pa11y-reports/logg-inn.txt || true
      - name: Upload pa11y results
        if: always()
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@cf430e030ddbb5b0abf93d22962f4752f3646cd9 # v7
        with:
          name: pa11y-reports
          path: pa11y-reports/
      - name: Check for accessibility issues
        run: |
          if grep -q "Error:" pa11y-reports/*.txt; then
            echo "Accessibility issues found. Check the uploaded artifacts for details."
            exit 1
          fi
```

## File: .github/workflows/playwright.yml
```yaml
name: Playwright Tests
on:
  push:
    branches: [main, master]
  pull_request:
    branches: [main, master]
permissions:
  contents: read
jobs:
  test:
    timeout-minutes: 60
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@2e223e0f0d2b8fd9872cbadb8b7428e5f8b5556d
      - uses: actions/setup-node@949feb2413d6458794dcd2491c4babbbce0c15c1 # v7
        with:
          node-version: 24
          cache: "pnpm"
      - name: Install dependencies
        run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts
      - name: Install Playwright Browsers
        run: pnpm exec playwright install --with-deps # NOSONAR - playwright install downloads browser binaries, not npm packages
      - name: Build the project
        run: pnpm run build
        env:
          NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL: ${{ secrets.NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL }}
      - name: Start the application
        run: pnpm run start &
        env:
          NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL: ${{ secrets.NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL }}
      - name: Wait for the application to be ready
        run: |
          echo "Waiting for the application to be ready..."
          timeout 300 bash -c 'while [[ "$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w ''%{http_code}'' localhost:3000)" != "200" ]]; do sleep 5; done' || false
          echo "Application is ready!"
      - name: Run Playwright tests
        run: pnpm exec playwright test
        env:
          CI: true
          NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL: ${{ secrets.NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL }}
          DEBUG: pw:api
      - name: Upload test results
        if: always()
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@cf430e030ddbb5b0abf93d22962f4752f3646cd9 # v7
        with:
          name: playwright-report
          path: playwright-report/
          retention-days: 30
      - name: Upload test traces
        if: failure()
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@cf430e030ddbb5b0abf93d22962f4752f3646cd9 # v7
        with:
          name: playwright-traces
          path: test-results/
          retention-days: 30
```

## File: .github/workflows/react-doctor.yml
```yaml
name: React Doctor
on:
  push:
    branches: [main, master]
  pull_request:
    branches: [main, master]
concurrency:
  group: ${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}
  cancel-in-progress: true
jobs:
  react-doctor:
    name: React Health Check
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 30
    permissions:
      contents: read
      pull-requests: write
      issues: write
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7
      - name: Detect package manager
        id: pm
        run: |
          if [ -f "pnpm-lock.yaml" ]; then
            echo "manager=pnpm" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            echo "install=pnpm install --frozen-lockfile" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            echo "run=pnpm run" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            echo "cache=pnpm" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            echo "Detected pnpm (pnpm-lock.yaml found)"
          elif [ -f "yarn.lock" ]; then
            echo "manager=yarn" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            echo "install=yarn install --frozen-lockfile" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            echo "run=yarn" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            echo "cache=yarn" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            echo "Detected yarn (yarn.lock found)"
          else
            echo "manager=npm" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            echo "install=npm ci" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            echo "run=npm run" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            echo "cache=npm" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            echo "Detected npm (default)"
          fi
      - name: Install pnpm
        if: steps.pm.outputs.manager == 'pnpm'
        uses: pnpm/action-setup@2e223e0f0d2b8fd9872cbadb8b7428e5f8b5556d
      - uses: actions/setup-node@949feb2413d6458794dcd2491c4babbbce0c15c1 # v7
        with:
          node-version: 24
          cache: ${{ steps.pm.outputs.cache }}
      - name: Install dependencies
        run: ${{ steps.pm.outputs.install }}
      - name: Run React Doctor
        id: doctor
        run: |
          # Unset CI env vars so react-doctor does NOT auto-enable offline mode.
          # This forces online scoring via the react.doctor API.
          # react-doctor checks: GITHUB_ACTIONS, GITLAB_CI, CIRCLECI, and CI === "true"
          unset CI GITHUB_ACTIONS GITLAB_CI CIRCLECI
          # Run react-doctor via local devDependency (avoids npx isolation issues with typescript)
          ${{ steps.pm.outputs.run }} doctor 2>&1 | tee /tmp/react-doctor-raw.txt
          # Strip ANSI escape codes for clean output
          sed 's/\x1b\[[0-9;]*m//g; s/\x1b\[[0-9;]*[A-Za-z]//g' /tmp/react-doctor-raw.txt | tr -d '\r' > /tmp/react-doctor-output.txt
          # Check for lint issues (the actionable part of the report)
          if grep -qi 'No issues found' /tmp/react-doctor-output.txt; then
            echo "issues_found=false" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
          else
            echo "issues_found=true" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
          fi
          # Extract score from the cleaned output (matches "96 / 100" pattern)
          SCORE=$(grep -oP '\d+ / 100' /tmp/react-doctor-output.txt | head -1 || echo "unknown")
          echo "score=$SCORE" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
          # Extract just the numeric score for threshold check
          SCORE_NUM=$(echo "$SCORE" | grep -oP '^\d+' || echo "0")
          echo "score_num=$SCORE_NUM" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
          echo "Detected score: $SCORE (numeric: $SCORE_NUM)"
      - name: Post PR comment with results
        if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'
        uses: actions/github-script@3a2844b7e9c422d3c10d287c895573f7108da1b3 # v9
        with:
          script: |
            const fs = require('fs');
            const output = fs.readFileSync('/tmp/react-doctor-output.txt', 'utf8');
            const score = '${{ steps.doctor.outputs.score }}';
            const offline = '${{ steps.doctor.outputs.offline }}' === 'true';
            const issuesFound = '${{ steps.doctor.outputs.issues_found }}' === 'true';
            const scoreDisplay = offline
              ? 'N/A (scoring server unreachable)'
              : score;
            const statusEmoji = offline
              ? (issuesFound ? '⚠️' : '✅')
              : '';
            const body = [
              `## 🩺 React Doctor — Score: ${scoreDisplay} ${statusEmoji}`,
              '',
              offline ? '> ⚠️ Scoring server was unreachable in CI. Lint results are still valid.' : '',
              '',
              '<details>',
              '<summary>Full diagnostics</summary>',
              '',
              '```',
              output.trim(),
              '```',
              '',
              '</details>',
              '',
              '> Powered by [react-doctor](https://github.com/millionco/react-doctor)',
            ].filter(Boolean).join('\n');
            // Find existing comment to update instead of creating duplicates
            const { data: comments } = await github.rest.issues.listComments({
              owner: context.repo.owner,
              repo: context.repo.repo,
              issue_number: context.issue.number,
            });
            const botComment = comments.find(
              (c) => c.body?.includes('🩺 React Doctor')
            );
            if (botComment) {
              await github.rest.issues.updateComment({
                owner: context.repo.owner,
                repo: context.repo.repo,
                comment_id: botComment.id,
                body,
              });
            } else {
              await github.rest.issues.createComment({
                owner: context.repo.owner,
                repo: context.repo.repo,
                issue_number: context.issue.number,
                body,
              });
            }
      - name: Write job summary
        if: always()
        run: |
          OFFLINE="false"
          if [ "$OFFLINE" = "true" ]; then
            echo "## 🩺 React Doctor — Score: N/A (scoring server unreachable)" >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
            echo "" >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
            echo "> ⚠️ Scoring server was unreachable in CI. Lint results are still valid." >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
          else
            echo "## 🩺 React Doctor — Score: ${{ steps.doctor.outputs.score }}" >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
          fi
          echo "" >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
          echo '```' >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
          cat /tmp/react-doctor-output.txt >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
          echo '```' >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
      - name: Check score threshold
        run: |
          OFFLINE="false"
          ISSUES="${{ steps.doctor.outputs.issues_found }}"
          # When offline, skip numeric threshold — only fail if lint issues were found
          if [ "$OFFLINE" = "true" ]; then
            echo "⚠️ Scoring server unreachable — skipping numeric threshold check"
            if [ "$ISSUES" = "true" ]; then
              echo "::error::react-doctor found lint issues (score unavailable due to offline)"
              exit 1
            fi
            echo "✅ No lint issues found (score unavailable due to offline)"
            exit 0
          fi
          SCORE="${{ steps.doctor.outputs.score_num }}"
          SCORE="${SCORE:-0}"
          THRESHOLD=80
          echo "React Doctor score: $SCORE (threshold: $THRESHOLD)"
          if [ "$SCORE" -lt "$THRESHOLD" ]; then
            echo "::error::React Doctor score $SCORE is below threshold $THRESHOLD"
            exit 1
          fi
```

## File: .github/workflows/repomix.yml
```yaml
name: Repository Documentation
on:
  push:
    branches:
      - main
  workflow_dispatch: # allows manual triggering
permissions:
  contents: write
  pull-requests: write
jobs:
  analyze:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Checkout repository
        uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0 # fetch all history for better context
      - name: Wait for other checks
        run: |
          echo "Waiting for 5 minutes to allow other checks to complete..."
          sleep 300
      - name: Setup Node.js
        uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with:
          node-version: "24"
      - name: Install Repomix
        run: npm install -g repomix@0.2.12 --ignore-scripts
      - name: Generate Repository Documentation
        run: |
          echo "Creating DOCS directory..."
          mkdir -p DOCS
          echo "Running Repomix..."
          if ! repomix --output DOCS/repository_context.txt --style markdown --remove-empty-lines --verbose; then
            echo "Error: Repomix command failed"
            # Print directory contents for debugging
            echo "DOCS directory contents:"
            ls -la DOCS/
            exit 1
          fi
          echo "Verifying output file..."
          if [ ! -f "DOCS/repository_context.txt" ]; then
            echo "Error: repository_context.txt was not created"
            # Print directory contents for debugging
            echo "DOCS directory contents:"
            ls -la DOCS/
            exit 1
          fi
          if [ ! -s "DOCS/repository_context.txt" ]; then
            echo "Error: repository_context.txt is empty"
            exit 1
          fi
          echo "Repository context file generated successfully"
          echo "File size: $(stat --format=%s "DOCS/repository_context.txt") bytes"
          echo "First few lines of the file:"
          head -n 5 "DOCS/repository_context.txt"
      # Update Documentation
      - name: Commit and Push Changes
        run: |
          echo "Configuring git..."
          git config --local user.email "github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com"
          git config --local user.name "github-actions[bot]"
          echo "Checking for changes..."
          if [[ -n "$(git status --porcelain)" ]]; then
            echo "Changes detected, committing..."
            # Stage only repository_context.txt to avoid unintended changes
            if ! git add DOCS/repository_context.txt; then
              echo "Error: Failed to stage repository_context.txt"
              exit 1
            fi
            if ! git commit -m "docs: update repository context via Repomix [skip ci]"; then
              echo "Error: Failed to create commit"
              exit 1
            fi
            echo "Pushing to main branch..."
            if ! git push; then
              echo "Error: Failed to push changes"
              exit 1
            fi
            echo "Successfully updated repository context"
          else
            echo "No changes detected in repository_context.txt"
          fi
```

## File: .github/workflows/security.yml
```yaml
name: OWASP Security Scan
on:
  pull_request:
  push:
    branches: [main, master]
permissions:
  contents: read
jobs:
  secrets:
    name: Secret Detection
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: read
      pull-requests: write
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0
      - name: Run gitleaks
        uses: gitleaks/gitleaks-action@e0c47f4f8be36e29cdc102c57e68cb5cbf0e8d1e # v3.0.0
        env:
          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
  sast:
    name: SAST - Semgrep
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: read
    container:
      image: semgrep/semgrep:1.181.0
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1
      - name: Run Semgrep with OWASP rules
        run: |
          semgrep scan \
            --config .semgrep/ \
            --config "p/owasp-top-ten" \
            --config "p/javascript" \
            --config "p/typescript" \
            --config "p/react" \
            --severity ERROR \
            --error \
            --verbose
        # --config .semgrep/         → Custom rules (owasp-top10, react, nextjs)
        # --config "p/owasp-top-ten" → Semgrep registry OWASP rules (auto-updated)
        # --config "p/javascript"    → Semgrep registry JS rules (auto-updated)
        # --config "p/typescript"    → Semgrep registry TS rules (auto-updated)
        # --config "p/react"         → Semgrep registry React rules (auto-updated)
        # --severity ERROR           → Only report ERROR-level findings (ignore WARNINGs)
        # --error                    → Exit code 1 if findings (fails the job)
  dependencies:
    name: Dependency Scan
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: read
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1
      - name: Run Trivy vulnerability scanner
        uses: aquasecurity/trivy-action@ed142fd0673e97e23eac54620cfb913e5ce36c25 # v0.36.0
        with:
          scan-type: fs
          scan-ref: .
          severity: CRITICAL,HIGH
          exit-code: 1
          format: table
```

rules:
  # ============================================================
  # Next.js Security Rules
  # ============================================================
  # ============================================================
  # OWASP A01:2021 - Broken Access Control
  # ============================================================
  - id: nextjs-api-route-no-method-check
    patterns:
      - pattern: |
          export default function $HANDLER($REQ, $RES) {
            ...
          }
      - pattern-not: |
          export default function $HANDLER($REQ, $RES) {
            ...
            $REQ.method ...
            ...
          }
    message: >
      Next.js API route does not check req.method. Without method
      validation, the endpoint accepts GET, POST, PUT, DELETE etc.
      Check req.method and return 405 for unsupported methods.
      OWASP A01:2021 - Broken Access Control.
    severity: WARNING
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    paths:
      include:
        - "**/pages/api/**"
        - "**/app/api/**"
    metadata:
      owasp: A01:2021
      cwe: CWE-749
  - id: nextjs-api-route-no-auth-check
    patterns:
      - pattern: |
          export default function $HANDLER($REQ, $RES) {
            ...
          }
      - pattern-not: |
          export default function $HANDLER($REQ, $RES) {
            ...
            getSession(...)
            ...
          }
      - pattern-not: |
          export default function $HANDLER($REQ, $RES) {
            ...
            getToken(...)
            ...
          }
      - pattern-not: |
          export default function $HANDLER($REQ, $RES) {
            ...
            getServerSession(...)
            ...
          }
      - pattern-not: |
          export default function $HANDLER($REQ, $RES) {
            ...
            auth(...)
            ...
          }
      - pattern-not: |
          export default function $HANDLER($REQ, $RES) {
            ...
            $REQ.headers.authorization ...
            ...
          }
    message: >
      Next.js API route without visible authentication check.
      Verify the user's session or token before processing.
      OWASP A01:2021 - Broken Access Control.
    severity: WARNING
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    paths:
      include:
        - "**/pages/api/**"
        - "**/app/api/**"
    metadata:
      owasp: A01:2021
      cwe: CWE-862
  # ============================================================
  # OWASP A03:2021 - Injection
  # ============================================================
  - id: nextjs-dangerously-set-inner-html
    patterns:
      - pattern: |
          <$EL dangerouslySetInnerHTML={...} />
      - pattern-not: |
          <$EL dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{__html: DOMPurify.sanitize(...)}} />
      - pattern-not: |
          <$EL dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{__html: sanitize(...)}} />
      - pattern-not: |
          <$EL dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{__html: purify.sanitize(...)}} />
    message: >
      dangerouslySetInnerHTML in Next.js page without sanitization.
      SSR makes this especially dangerous — XSS payloads are rendered
      server-side and served to all users. Use DOMPurify.
      OWASP A03:2021 - Injection.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    paths:
      include:
        - "**/pages/**"
        - "**/app/**"
        - "**/components/**"
    metadata:
      owasp: A03:2021
      cwe: CWE-79
  # ============================================================
  # OWASP A05:2021 - Security Misconfiguration
  # ============================================================
  - id: nextjs-env-client-exposure
    patterns:
      - pattern-regex: NEXT_PUBLIC_(SECRET|PASSWORD|API_KEY|PRIVATE|JWT_SECRET|DATABASE|DB_)
    message: >
      Sensitive-looking env variable uses NEXT_PUBLIC_ prefix, which
      exposes it to the browser bundle. Remove NEXT_PUBLIC_ prefix
      for server-only secrets and access them only in API routes
      or getServerSideProps.
      OWASP A05:2021 - Security Misconfiguration.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [generic]
    paths:
      include:
        - "**/.env*"
        - "**/next.config.*"
    metadata:
      owasp: A05:2021
      cwe: CWE-200
  - id: nextjs-missing-security-headers
    patterns:
      - pattern: |
          const $CONFIG = { ... }
      - pattern-not: |
          const $CONFIG = { ..., headers: ..., ... }
    message: >
      next.config.js without security headers configuration. Add
      headers() to set X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options,
      Referrer-Policy, and Content-Security-Policy.
      OWASP A05:2021 - Security Misconfiguration.
    severity: WARNING
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    paths:
      include:
        - "**/next.config.*"
    metadata:
      owasp: A05:2021
      cwe: CWE-693
  - id: nextjs-image-remote-wildcard
    patterns:
      - pattern-regex: "remotePatterns.*\\*\\*"
    message: >
      Next.js Image component configured with wildcard remote patterns.
      Restrict to specific domains to prevent SSRF through image optimization.
      OWASP A05:2021 - Security Misconfiguration.
    severity: WARNING
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    paths:
      include:
        - "**/next.config.*"
    metadata:
      owasp: A05:2021
      cwe: CWE-918
  # ============================================================
  # OWASP A07:2021 - Authentication Failures
  # ============================================================
  - id: nextjs-jwt-localstorage
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem("token", ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem('token', ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem("jwt", ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem('jwt', ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem("access_token", ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem('access_token', ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem("accessToken", ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem('accessToken', ...)
    message: >
      Storing auth tokens in localStorage in a Next.js app. Use
      httpOnly cookies with next-auth or similar. Next.js has
      server-side capabilities — leverage them for secure auth.
      OWASP A07:2021 - Identification and Authentication Failures.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A07:2021
      cwe: CWE-922
  # ============================================================
  # OWASP A08:2021 - Data Integrity Failures
  # ============================================================
  - id: nextjs-api-route-error-exposure
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: |
              $RES.status(500).json({ ..., error: $ERR.message, ... })
          - pattern: |
              $RES.status(500).json({ ..., stack: $ERR.stack, ... })
          - pattern: |
              $RES.status(500).json({ ..., error: $ERR, ... })
          - pattern: $RES.status(500).send($ERR.message)
          - pattern: $RES.status(500).send($ERR.stack)
    message: >
      API route exposes error details to the client. Return a generic
      error message and log the full error server-side.
      OWASP A09:2021 - Security Logging and Monitoring Failures.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    paths:
      include:
        - "**/pages/api/**"
        - "**/app/api/**"
    metadata:
      owasp: A09:2021
      cwe: CWE-209
  # ============================================================
  # OWASP A10:2021 - SSRF
  # ============================================================
  - id: nextjs-ssrf-in-server-props
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: |
              export async function getServerSideProps($CTX) {
                ...
                fetch($CTX.query.$PARAM)
                ...
              }
          - pattern: |
              export async function getServerSideProps($CTX) {
                ...
                axios.get($CTX.query.$PARAM)
                ...
              }
    message: >
      URL from query parameters passed to fetch inside getServerSideProps.
      This runs server-side and can be exploited for SSRF. Validate
      URLs against an allowlist.
      OWASP A10:2021 - Server-Side Request Forgery.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    paths:
      include:
        - "**/pages/**"
    metadata:
      owasp: A10:2021
      cwe: CWE-918
```

## File: .semgrep/owasp-top10.yml
```yaml
rules:
  # ============================================================
  # A01:2021 - Broken Access Control
  # ============================================================
  # ============================================================
  # A02:2021 - Cryptographic Failures
  # ============================================================
  - id: weak-hash-md5
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: crypto.createHash("md5")
          - pattern: crypto.createHash('md5')
          - pattern: CryptoJS.MD5(...)
    message: >
      MD5 is cryptographically broken and should not be used for security
      purposes. Use SHA-256 or bcrypt for hashing.
      OWASP A02:2021 - Cryptographic Failures.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A02:2021
      cwe: CWE-328
  - id: weak-hash-sha1
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: crypto.createHash("sha1")
          - pattern: crypto.createHash('sha1')
          - pattern: CryptoJS.SHA1(...)
    message: >
      SHA-1 is deprecated for security use. Use SHA-256 or stronger.
      OWASP A02:2021 - Cryptographic Failures.
    severity: WARNING
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A02:2021
      cwe: CWE-328
  - id: hardcoded-secret-in-code
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: |
              const $SECRET = "..."
          - pattern: |
              let $SECRET = "..."
          - pattern: |
              var $SECRET = "..."
      - metavariable-regex:
          metavariable: $SECRET
          regex: (?i)(password|secret|apiKey|api_key|token|privateKey|private_key|SECRET_KEY|JWT_SECRET)
    message: >
      Possible hardcoded secret assigned to variable '$SECRET'.
      Use environment variables or a secrets manager.
      OWASP A02:2021 - Cryptographic Failures.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A02:2021
      cwe: CWE-798
  # ============================================================
  # A03:2021 - Injection
  # ============================================================
  - id: sql-string-concatenation
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: $DB.query(`...${...}...`)
          - pattern: $DB.query("..." + ...)
          - pattern: $DB.raw(`...${...}...`)
          - pattern: $DB.raw("..." + ...)
          - pattern: |
              $QUERY = `...${...}...`
              ...
              $DB.query($QUERY)
    message: >
      SQL query built with string concatenation or interpolation.
      Use parameterized queries to prevent SQL injection.
      OWASP A03:2021 - Injection.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A03:2021
      cwe: CWE-89
  - id: command-injection-exec
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: child_process.exec(`...${...}...`)
          - pattern: child_process.exec("..." + ...)
          - pattern: exec(`...${...}...`)
          - pattern: require('child_process').exec(`...${...}...`)
    message: >
      User input may reach child_process.exec, allowing command injection.
      Use child_process.execFile with an argument array instead.
      OWASP A03:2021 - Injection.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A03:2021
      cwe: CWE-78
  # ============================================================
  # A04:2021 - Insecure Design
  # ============================================================
  - id: eval-usage
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: eval(...)
          - pattern: new Function(...)
    message: >
      eval() and new Function() execute arbitrary code and are a major
      security risk. Refactor to avoid dynamic code execution.
      OWASP A04:2021 - Insecure Design.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A04:2021
      cwe: CWE-95
  - id: settimeout-string-arg
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: setTimeout("...", ...)
          - pattern: setInterval("...", ...)
    message: >
      setTimeout/setInterval with a string argument acts like eval().
      Pass a function reference instead.
      OWASP A04:2021 - Insecure Design.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A04:2021
      cwe: CWE-95
  # ============================================================
  # A05:2021 - Security Misconfiguration
  # ============================================================
  - id: cors-wildcard-origin
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: |
              cors({ origin: "*" })
          - pattern: |
              cors({ origin: '*' })
          - pattern: |
              cors({ origin: true })
          - pattern: $RES.setHeader("Access-Control-Allow-Origin", "*")
          - pattern: $RES.setHeader('Access-Control-Allow-Origin', '*')
    message: >
      CORS configured with wildcard or unrestricted origin.
      Whitelist specific origins in production.
      OWASP A05:2021 - Security Misconfiguration.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A05:2021
      cwe: CWE-942
  - id: debug-mode-enabled
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: |
              debug: true
          - pattern: |
              DEBUG = true
          - pattern: |
              NODE_ENV === "development"
    message: >
      Debug mode or development check detected. Ensure this is not
      active in production builds.
      OWASP A05:2021 - Security Misconfiguration.
    severity: WARNING
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A05:2021
      cwe: CWE-489
  # ============================================================
  # A07:2021 - Identification and Authentication Failures
  # ============================================================
  - id: jwt-no-verify
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: jwt.decode(...)
    pattern-not: jwt.verify(...)
    message: >
      jwt.decode() does NOT verify the signature. Use jwt.verify()
      to validate tokens before trusting their contents.
      OWASP A07:2021 - Authentication Failures.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A07:2021
      cwe: CWE-345
  - id: insecure-cookie-options
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: |
              { ..., httpOnly: false, ... }
          - pattern: |
              { ..., secure: false, ... }
    message: >
      Cookies should have httpOnly and secure flags set to true.
      httpOnly prevents XSS access, secure ensures HTTPS-only transmission.
      OWASP A07:2021 - Authentication Failures.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A07:2021
      cwe: CWE-614
  # ============================================================
  # A08:2021 - Software and Data Integrity Failures
  # ============================================================
  - id: open-redirect
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: res.redirect($REQ.query.$PARAM)
          - pattern: res.redirect($REQ.body.$PARAM)
          - pattern: res.redirect($REQ.params.$PARAM)
    message: >
      Redirecting to a URL from user input without validation enables
      open redirect attacks. Validate against an allowlist of URLs.
      OWASP A08:2021 - Software and Data Integrity Failures.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A08:2021
      cwe: CWE-601
  # ============================================================
  # A09:2021 - Security Logging and Monitoring Failures
  # ============================================================
  - id: logging-sensitive-data
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: console.log(..., $OBJ.password, ...)
          - pattern: console.log(..., $OBJ.token, ...)
          - pattern: console.log(..., $OBJ.secret, ...)
          - pattern: console.log(..., $OBJ.apiKey, ...)
          - pattern: console.log(..., $OBJ.creditCard, ...)
          - pattern: |
              console.log(`...password...`)
          - pattern: |
              console.log(`...token...`)
    message: >
      Sensitive data (passwords, tokens, secrets) should never appear in logs.
      OWASP A09:2021 - Security Logging and Monitoring Failures.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A09:2021
      cwe: CWE-532
  # ============================================================
  # A10:2021 - Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF)
  # ============================================================
  - id: ssrf-fetch-user-input
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: fetch($REQ.query.$URL)
          - pattern: fetch($REQ.body.$URL)
          - pattern: fetch($REQ.params.$URL)
          - pattern: axios.get($REQ.query.$URL)
          - pattern: axios.get($REQ.body.$URL)
          - pattern: axios.post($REQ.body.$URL, ...)
    message: >
      URL from user input passed directly to HTTP client.
      Validate and whitelist allowed URLs to prevent SSRF.
      OWASP A10:2021 - Server-Side Request Forgery.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A10:2021
      cwe: CWE-918
```

## File: .semgrep/react-security.yml
```yaml
rules:
  # ============================================================
  # React Security Rules
  # OWASP A03:2021 - Injection / XSS Prevention
  # ============================================================
  - id: react-dangerously-set-inner-html
    patterns:
      - pattern: |
          <$EL dangerouslySetInnerHTML={...} />
      - pattern-not: |
          <$EL dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{__html: DOMPurify.sanitize(...)}} />
      - pattern-not: |
          <$EL dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{__html: purify.sanitize(...)}} />
      - pattern-not: |
          <$EL dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{__html: sanitize(...)}} />
    message: >
      dangerouslySetInnerHTML used without DOMPurify sanitization.
      This is a direct XSS vector. Sanitize HTML with DOMPurify before rendering.
      OWASP A03:2021 - Injection.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A03:2021
      cwe: CWE-79
  - id: react-href-javascript-protocol
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: <a href="javascript:..." ...>...</a>
          - pattern: <a href={'javascript:...'} ...>...</a>
          - pattern: <a href={`javascript:...`} ...>...</a>
    message: >
      javascript: protocol in href is an XSS vector.
      Use onClick handlers or validated URLs instead.
      OWASP A03:2021 - Injection.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A03:2021
      cwe: CWE-79
  - id: react-ref-innerhtml
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: $REF.current.innerHTML = ...
          - pattern: $EL.innerHTML = ...
    message: >
      Direct innerHTML assignment bypasses React's XSS protection.
      Use React state and dangerouslySetInnerHTML with DOMPurify,
      or better yet, render content through React components.
      OWASP A03:2021 - Injection.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A03:2021
      cwe: CWE-79
  # ============================================================
  # OWASP A07:2021 - Authentication Failures
  # ============================================================
  - id: react-jwt-localstorage
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem("token", ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem('token', ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem("jwt", ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem('jwt', ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem("access_token", ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem('access_token', ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem("accessToken", ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem('accessToken', ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem("auth_token", ...)
          - pattern: localStorage.setItem('auth_token', ...)
    message: >
      Storing auth tokens in localStorage is vulnerable to XSS theft.
      Use httpOnly cookies set by the server instead.
      OWASP A07:2021 - Identification and Authentication Failures.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A07:2021
      cwe: CWE-922
  # ============================================================
  # OWASP A08:2021 - Software and Data Integrity Failures
  # ============================================================
  - id: react-postmessage-no-origin-check
    pattern: |
      window.addEventListener("message", function($EVENT) {
        ...
      })
    pattern-not: |
      window.addEventListener("message", function($EVENT) {
        ...
        $EVENT.origin ...
        ...
      })
    message: >
      postMessage listener without origin validation. Always check
      event.origin against a trusted allowlist to prevent cross-origin attacks.
      OWASP A08:2021 - Software and Data Integrity Failures.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A08:2021
      cwe: CWE-346
  - id: react-window-open-noopener
    patterns:
      - pattern: window.open($URL)
      - pattern-not: window.open($URL, $NAME, "...noopener...")
    message: >
      window.open without 'noopener' allows the opened page to access
      window.opener. Add 'noopener,noreferrer' to the features parameter.
      OWASP A08:2021 - Software and Data Integrity Failures.
    severity: WARNING
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A08:2021
      cwe: CWE-1022
  # ============================================================
  # OWASP A04:2021 - Insecure Design
  # ============================================================
  - id: react-eval-in-component
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: |
              function $COMP(...) {
                ...
                eval(...)
                ...
              }
          - pattern: |
              const $COMP = (...) => {
                ...
                eval(...)
                ...
              }
    message: >
      eval() inside a React component is extremely dangerous.
      User input could reach eval through props or state.
      Refactor to avoid dynamic code execution entirely.
      OWASP A04:2021 - Insecure Design.
    severity: ERROR
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A04:2021
      cwe: CWE-95
  # ============================================================
  # OWASP A05:2021 - Security Misconfiguration
  # ============================================================
  - id: react-insecure-fetch-http
    patterns:
      - pattern-either:
          - pattern: fetch("http://...")
          - pattern: fetch('http://...')
          - pattern: axios.get("http://...")
          - pattern: axios.get('http://...')
          - pattern: axios.post("http://...", ...)
          - pattern: axios.post('http://...', ...)
    message: >
      HTTP request to non-HTTPS URL. All API calls should use HTTPS
      to prevent data interception.
      OWASP A05:2021 - Security Misconfiguration.
    severity: WARNING
    languages: [javascript, typescript]
    metadata:
      owasp: A05:2021
      cwe: CWE-319
```

## File: DOCS/architecture-review-2026-08-24.html

          <p><strong>Problem:</strong> the Cart has no module — fetch, format, sync, and the "wait 2–3s for WooCommerce to settle" retry hack are copy-pasted across four components.</p>
          <p><strong>Solution:</strong> one <code class="font-mono text-sm">useCart()</code> interface; query, mutation, formatting, settle-refetch, and store sync become implementation.</p>
          <ul class="list-disc list-inside text-sm space-y-1">
            <li>locality: cart bugs concentrate in one module</li>
            <li>leverage: one interface, four call sites</li>
            <li>tests hit one interface</li>
            <li>settle-hack fixed once, everywhere</li>
            <li>delete 4 copies of the ritual</li>
          </ul>

                <p class="module-label text-emerald-300">Cart module (candidate 1)</p>
                <p class="text-[10px] font-mono text-slate-300 opacity-60">getFormattedCart · getUpdatedItems · handleQuantityChange</p>
              </div>
              <div class="deep rounded p-3 text-white">
                <p class="module-label text-emerald-300">Checkout module</p>
                <p class="text-[10px] font-mono text-slate-300 opacity-60">createCheckoutData</p>
              </div>
              <div class="border-2 border-slate-400 rounded p-3">
                <p class="module-label">price formatting</p>
                <p class="text-[10px] font-mono text-slate-500">paddedPrice · filteredVariantPrice · trimmedString</p>
              </div>
              <p class="text-xs text-slate-400 line-through">type re-exports — deleted, import from @/types</p>
            </div>
          </div>
          <p><strong>Problem:</strong> <code class="font-mono text-sm">handleQuantityChange</code> was extracted for testability, but the bugs live in the mutation/refetch dance around it — no locality; the rest is five concerns sharing a filename.</p>
          <p><strong>Solution:</strong> each function moves into the module whose implementation it belongs to; the re-export shim is deleted.</p>
          <ul class="list-disc list-inside text-sm space-y-1">
            <li>locality: cart shaping tested inside the Cart module</li>
            <li>delete the backward-compat shim</li>
            <li>deletion test passes: file vanishes, nothing orphaned</li>
          </ul>
        </article>

flowchart LR
  L[login] --> C1[shared client]
  App[everything else] --> C1
  C1 --> MW[session middleware + afterware]
  MW --> WP[WPGraphQL]
  classDef deepM fill:#0f172a,color:#fff,stroke:#0f172a;
  class C1,MW deepM
              </pre>
            </div>

          <p><strong>Problem:</strong> <code class="font-mono text-sm">login()</code> constructs a second ApolloClient that bypasses the shared client's session middleware — the woocommerce-session header never rides along with login.</p>
          <p><strong>Solution:</strong> login uses the shared client; if isolation was intentional, that reason belongs in an ADR, not in an unexplained second client.</p>
          <ul class="list-disc list-inside text-sm space-y-1">
            <li>one seam to WPGraphQL, not two</li>
            <li>session behaviour uniform across all requests</li>
          </ul>
          <div class="rounded border border-amber-300 bg-amber-50 px-3 py-2 text-xs text-amber-800">
            Speculative: the second client may be deliberate (avoid attaching a guest session to login). If so, record the reason as an ADR.
          </div>
        </article>
      </section>
      <!-- ============ TOP RECOMMENDATION ============ -->
      <section id="top-recommendation" class="rounded-xl deep text-white p-8">
        <p class="module-label text-emerald-300 mb-2">Top recommendation</p>
        <h2 class="font-serif text-2xl font-semibold mb-2">
          <a href="#c1" class="underline decoration-emerald-400 underline-offset-4">Deepen the Cart into one module</a>
        </h2>
        <p class="text-slate-300 text-sm max-w-2xl">
          It has the most leverage — one interface replaces four copies of the fetch→format→sync→settle ritual — and it pulls candidates 3 and 4 in behind it for free: the store interface shrinks and the grab-bag dissolves as side effects of the same deepening.
        </p>
      </section>
    </main>

## File: DOCS/INTERVIEW_READINESS_REVIEW.md
```markdown
# Interview Readiness Review: nextjs-woocommerce

## Post-Fix Assessment: 🟢 Solid — Ready with Minor Polish

After addressing the 6 critical issues, the codebase is in significantly better shape. The remaining items are quality improvements rather than interview red flags.

---

## ✅ Previously Critical — Now Fixed

| # | Issue | Status |
|---|-------|--------|
| 1 | Shared object reference bug in [`getFormattedCart()`](src/utils/functions/functions.tsx:145) | ✅ Fixed — each product is now a new object per iteration |
| 2 | Deprecated `process.browser` in 3 files | ✅ Fixed — all use `typeof window !== 'undefined'` |
| 3 | [`ApolloClient.js`](src/utils/apollo/ApolloClient.ts) was untyped JS with misleading naming | ✅ Converted to TypeScript with `SessionData` interface, `isServerSide` naming |
| 4 | Hardcoded `transactionId: 'fhggdfjgfi'` | ✅ Fixed — now uses `uuidv4()` |
| 5 | `setTimeout` refetch calls | ✅ Kept with explanatory comments — required by WooCommerce backend latency; added `refetchQueries` to AddToCart mutation |
| 6 | README version mismatch | ✅ Updated to Next.js 16.1.6 / React 19.2.4 |

---

## 🟡 Remaining Improvements (Phase 2 — Code Quality)

### 1. Stale Closure in `onCompleted` Callbacks

**Files:** [`AddToCart.component.tsx:112`](src/components/Product/AddToCart.component.tsx:112), [`CartContents.component.tsx:29`](src/components/Cart/CartContents.component.tsx:29), [`CheckoutForm.component.tsx:62`](src/components/Checkout/CheckoutForm.component.tsx:62)

The `onCompleted` callback references `data` from the outer scope instead of using the callback parameter:

```typescript
// Current — data may be stale
onCompleted: () => {
  const updatedCart = getFormattedCart(data);
}

// Better — use the fresh data from the callback
onCompleted: (freshData) => {
  const updatedCart = getFormattedCart(freshData);
}
```

**Risk:** Low-to-medium. Works in practice because `notifyOnNetworkStatusChange: true` forces re-renders, but it's technically incorrect and an interviewer who knows Apollo well could question it.

### 2. Duplicate Type Definitions

Types are defined multiple times instead of being shared from a central location:
- `IImage` defined in [`functions.tsx:18`](src/utils/functions/functions.tsx:18), [`AddToCart.component.tsx:19`](src/components/Product/AddToCart.component.tsx:19), and [`DisplayProducts.component.tsx:7`](src/components/Product/DisplayProducts.component.tsx:7)
- `Product` interface exists in both [`cartStore.ts:4`](src/stores/cartStore.ts:4) and [`product.ts:32`](src/types/product.ts:32) with different shapes
- Multiple `IProductRootObject` interfaces with different shapes

**Interview concern:** Shows this hasn't been refactored yet. Consolidating to `src/types/` would demonstrate attention to DRY principles.

### 3. `uuidv4()` Used as React Key

**File:** [`DisplayProducts.component.tsx:78`](src/components/Product/DisplayProducts.component.tsx:78)

```tsx
<div key={uuidv4()} className="group">
```

This generates a **new key every render**, defeating React's reconciliation algorithm and causing unnecessary DOM re-creation. Should use `slug` or `databaseId`.

### 4. Deprecated `next/image` Props

**File:** [`CartContents.component.tsx:105`](src/components/Cart/CartContents.component.tsx:105)

```tsx
<Image layout="fill" objectFit="cover" />
```

The `layout` and `objectFit` props were deprecated in Next.js 13+. With Next.js 16, these should use the `fill` prop and CSS/className for styling instead.

### 5. Inconsistent Image Component Usage

- [`ProductCard.component.tsx`](src/components/Product/ProductCard.component.tsx) — uses `next/image` ✅
- [`DisplayProducts.component.tsx`](src/components/Product/DisplayProducts.component.tsx) — uses raw `<img>` ❌
- [`SingleProduct.component.tsx`](src/components/Product/SingleProduct.component.tsx) — uses raw `<img>` ❌
- ESLint rule `@next/next/no-img-element` is disabled in [`.eslintrc.json:7`](.eslintrc.json:7)

### 6. Missing Error Boundary

No React Error Boundary exists. If any component throws during render, the entire application crashes with no fallback UI.

### 7. Incomplete Authentication Implementation

**File:** [`auth.ts`](src/utils/auth.ts)

- [`hasCredentials()`](src/utils/auth.ts:5) always returns `false`
- [`getAuthToken()`](src/utils/auth.ts:15) always returns `null`
- [`logout()`](src/utils/auth.ts:85) only redirects without calling a logout mutation
- `REFRESH_AUTH_TOKEN` mutation in [`GQL_MUTATIONS.ts:40`](src/utils/gql/GQL_MUTATIONS.ts:40) is defined but never used
- `.env.example` has unused JWT-related keys

**Interview concern:** If asked about the auth flow, be prepared to explain this is cookie-based auth and these functions are stubs/placeholders.

### 8. Unused DevDependency

[`package.json:56`](package.json:56) includes `babel-plugin-styled-components` but styled-components isn't used anywhere in the project. The project uses Tailwind CSS for styling.

---

## 🔵 Phase 3 — Interview Polish (Nice-to-have)

### 9. Limited Test Coverage

Only 2 Playwright test files with 3 total tests:
- [`Index.spec.ts`](src/tests/Index/Index.spec.ts) — 1 test checking for h1 element
- [`Categories.spec.ts`](src/tests/Categories/Categories.spec.ts) — 2 tests for navigation

No unit tests exist for:
- Utility functions ([`getFormattedCart`](src/utils/functions/functions.tsx:145), [`filteredVariantPrice`](src/utils/functions/functions.tsx:132), [`paddedPrice`](src/utils/functions/functions.tsx:111))
- Custom hooks ([`useProductFilters`](src/hooks/useProductFilters.ts))
- Zustand store logic

Adding even 3-5 unit tests for the utility functions would significantly strengthen the testing story.

### 10. Import Organization in `functions.tsx`

**File:** [`functions.tsx:13`](src/utils/functions/functions.tsx:13)

The `ChangeEvent` import from React is placed between an interface declaration and a comment, instead of being grouped with other imports at the top.

### 11. `getUpdatedItems` Could Be Simplified

**File:** [`functions.tsx:237`](src/utils/functions/functions.tsx:237)

The current implementation uses `.forEach` + push. Could be simplified with `.map`:

```typescript
export const getUpdatedItems = (products, newQty, cartKey) =>
  products.map((item) => ({
    key: item.key,
    quantity: item.key === cartKey ? newQty : item.quantity,
  }));
```

# Project Review & Recommendations

## Overall Assessment

The project demonstrates solid engineering practices with a well-structured codebase. The separation of concerns is clear, components are modular, and the state management approach is pragmatic.

## Key Strengths

1. **Cart Implementation**
   - Clean separation between UI and state management
   - Effective use of React Context for global state
   - Smart persistence strategy with localStorage

2. **Checkout Flow**
   - Well-orchestrated process with clear state handling
   - Good use of react-hook-form for form management
   - Clear loading/error/success states

## Recommended Improvements

### 1. State Management Refinements
- Currently exposes raw `setCart` function in CartProvider, which could lead to inconsistent state
- Recommendation: Replace with specific action functions:
  ```typescript
  {
    addToCart: (product: Product) => void;
    removeFromCart: (productId: string) => void;
    updateQuantity: (productId: string, quantity: number) => void;
    clearCart: () => void;
  }
  ```

### 2. Security Enhancements
- Session tokens stored in localStorage are vulnerable to XSS
- Consider implementing:
  - HttpOnly cookies for session management
  - CSRF protection for mutations
  - Rate limiting on checkout endpoints

### 3. Payment System Flexibility
- Currently hardcoded to Cash on Delivery
- Suggested improvements:
  - Abstract payment method selection
  - Implement payment gateway integration interface
  - Add support for multiple payment providers

### 4. Error Handling
- Add comprehensive error boundaries
- Implement retry logic for failed GraphQL operations
- Add detailed error logging and monitoring
- Consider implementing offline support/queue for cart operations

### 5. Performance Optimizations
- Implement cart item quantity debouncing
- Add product list virtualization for large catalogs
- Consider implementing optimistic UI updates
- Add prefetching for common user paths

### 6. Developer Experience
- Add more comprehensive TypeScript types
- Consider implementing Storybook for component development
- Add unit tests for critical business logic
- Implement automated accessibility testing

### 7. User Experience
- Add toast notifications for cart operations
- Implement better loading skeletons
- Add offline support indicators
- Improve form error messaging and validation feedback

## Priority Recommendations

1. **High Priority**
   - Secure session management (move from localStorage to HttpOnly cookies)
   - Implement specific cart action functions instead of exposing setCart
   - Add comprehensive error handling

2. **Medium Priority**
   - Flexible payment gateway integration
   - Performance optimizations
   - Enhanced error feedback

3. **Nice to Have**
   - Developer experience improvements
   - Additional UX enhancements
   - Automated testing expansion
```

## File: DOCS/skill.md
```markdown
# Project Skill: nextjs-woocom

Read this first if you are an AI model working in this repository. For full source, see `DOCS/repository_context.txt` (generated, read-only).

## What this is
A headless eCommerce storefront. Next.js (Pages Router) + React + TypeScript frontend; WordPress + WooCommerce backend exposed through **WPGraphQL** and **WooGraphQL**. The UI language is **Norwegian** (routes, labels, error messages).

There is **no Algolia and no other external search service**. Product search runs against WooCommerce through WPGraphQL (`products(where: { search })`).

## Stack
| Area | Choice |
|---|---|
| Framework | Next.js 16 (Pages Router, `src/pages`), React 19, TypeScript |
| Data | `@apollo/client` against `NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL` |
| State | `zustand` (persisted cart store) |
| Forms | `react-hook-form` |
| Styling | Tailwind CSS 3 (+ small `src/styles/*.css`), `motion` for animation |
| Tests | Playwright (`src/tests/**`), Lighthouse CI, pa11y |
| Package manager | pnpm (npm lockfile also present); the repo's scripts use `pnpm exec` |

## Commands
- `pnpm dev` — `next dev --turbopack`
- `pnpm build` / `pnpm start`
- `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format` (prettier)
- `pnpm playwright` (also `:ui`, `:debug`, `:codegen`)
- `npx tsc --noEmit` — type check
- Build needs `NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL` set. Without it static pages fail with `Failed to parse URL from /graphql`.

## Environment (`.env.example`)
`NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_PLACEHOLDER_SMALL_IMAGE_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_PLACEHOLDER_LARGE_IMAGE_URL`, `NODE_ENV`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_AUTH_TOKEN_SS_KEY`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_REFRESH_TOKEN_LS_KEY`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SESSION_TOKEN_LS_KEY`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_AUTH_KEY_TIMEOUT`. Images must come from hosts allowed in `next.config.ts` `remotePatterns`.

## Layout
```
src/
  pages/          Routes (Norwegian names)
  components/     UI by feature: Cart, Category, Checkout, Footer, Header, Index,
                  Input, Layout, Product, ProductSearch, UI, User, Animations, SVG
  hooks/          useCartQuery, useProductFilters, useProductSearch
  stores/         cartStore.ts (zustand + persist)
  utils/
    apollo/ApolloClient.ts   Apollo client + session middleware/afterware
    gql/GQL_QUERIES.ts       All queries
    gql/GQL_MUTATIONS.ts     All mutations
    functions/               price.ts (formatPrice), productUtils.ts (filter/sort), functions.tsx
    constants/               LINKS.ts (nav), INPUT_FIELDS.ts (checkout fields)
    auth.ts                  Login helper + Norwegian error mapping
  types/          cart, checkout, graphql, product
  tests/          Playwright specs
wordpress/mu-plugins/headless-graphql-cors.php   CORS for the GraphQL endpoint
```

## Routes
| Route | File | Data fetching |
|---|---|---|
| `/` | `pages/index.tsx` | `getStaticProps`, revalidate 60 |
| `/produkter` | `pages/produkter.tsx` | `getStaticProps`, revalidate 60 |
| `/produkt/[slug]` | `pages/produkt/[slug].tsx` | `getServerSideProps` |
| `/kategorier` | `pages/kategorier.tsx` | `getStaticProps`, revalidate 10 |
| `/kategori/[slug]` | `pages/kategori/[slug].tsx` | `getServerSideProps` |
| `/handlekurv` | cart | client |
| `/kasse` | checkout | client |
| `/logg-inn`, `/min-konto` | login / account (`withAuth`) | client |

`pages/registrer.tsx.backup` is an inactive backup, not a route.

## Key flows
- **WooCommerce connection:** one Apollo client (`utils/apollo/ApolloClient.ts`). Middleware adds `woocommerce-session: Session <token>` from localStorage (key `woo-session:v1`, dropped after 7 days); afterware stores the token returned by WooGraphQL. Auth is cookie-based (`credentials: 'include'`).
- **Cart:** `cartStore` (zustand, persisted, key `woocommerce-cart:v1`) mirrors the server cart. `useCartQuery` runs `GET_CART`; `CartInitializer` seeds it. Mutations live in `GQL_MUTATIONS.ts`.
- **Product listing/filtering:** the product list is fetched once (`FETCH_ALL_PRODUCTS_QUERY`, first 50). Filtering and sorting by price, size, color and type happen **client-side** in `useProductFilters` and `utils/functions/productUtils.ts`.
- **Search:** `hooks/useProductSearch.ts` debounces input (300 ms), requires 2+ characters, and runs `SEARCH_PRODUCTS_QUERY` (8 results). UI is `components/ProductSearch/` (`SearchBox` desktop dropdown, `MobileSearch` inline, both built on `ProductSearch`; `SearchResultItem` renders a hit and links to `/produkt/<slug>`). Search quality depends on WordPress's default search, so a relevance plugin on the WP side improves it without frontend changes.
- **Prices:** always render through `formatPrice` (`nb-NO`, NOK, two decimals). WooGraphQL returns prices as strings that may include HTML or ranges.

## Product data shape
`Product` (`src/types/product.ts`): `databaseId, name, slug, onSale, price, regularPrice, salePrice?, image?.sourceUrl`, plus `productCategories`, `allPaColors`, `allPaSizes`, `variations.nodes` for variable products. Price fields exist only inside `... on SimpleProduct` / `... on VariableProduct` fragments, so new product queries must include those fragments.

## Conventions
- Components: `Name.component.tsx`, default export, PascalCase; hooks `useX.ts`; GraphQL documents as `UPPER_SNAKE` constants in `utils/gql`.
- Keep user-facing text Norwegian. Keep new routes Norwegian.
- Persisted client keys are versioned (`:v1`); bump the version if a stored schema changes.
- Prefer the shared UI pieces in `components/UI` and `components/Input`.
- Use `next/image` for images and keep hosts in `next.config.ts`.
- Prettier formatting; lint with `next lint`.

## When changing things
- New data → add the query/mutation in `utils/gql`, a type in `src/types`, and consume via Apollo hooks (client) or `client.query` in `getStaticProps`/`getServerSideProps` (server).
- Do not add a search service dependency without being asked; extend the WPGraphQL query instead.
- Never hardcode the GraphQL URL; use `NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL`.
- After changes, run `npx tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint`, and (with env set) `pnpm build`.

## Known gaps
- No `/sok` search results page (dropdown only).
- Full product list is capped at 50 and filtered on the client.
- `DOCS/repository_context.txt` is generated by Repomix (`.github/workflows/repomix.yml`: `repomix --output DOCS/repository_context.txt --style markdown --remove-empty-lines`). Regenerate it instead of hand-editing.
```

## File: DOCS/wordpress-cors.md
```markdown
# WordPress GraphQL CORS setup

The storefront sends credentialed requests to a different origin. The WordPress
GraphQL endpoint must explicitly allow the storefront origin; a wildcard is not
valid for credentialed requests. This cannot be fixed by Next.js response headers.

## Install on WordPress

1. Check whether a plugin, web server, or CDN already manages GraphQL CORS.
   If so, choose one implementation and remove conflicting rules. No existing
   CORS plugin is required to use this solution.
2. Identify the trusted frontend origins for this deployment as described below.
3. Upload [the must-use plugin](../wordpress/mu-plugins/headless-graphql-cors.php)
   to the WordPress server at
   `wp-content/mu-plugins/headless-graphql-cors.php`. Create the directory if needed.
   Place the file directly inside it; WordPress does not auto-load nested folders.
4. Must-use plugins load automatically. Verify it appears under Plugins → Must-Use.
5. Open **Settings → GraphQL CORS** and save the allowed storefront origins.
6. Clear any reverse-proxy/CDN cache. Avoid caching personalized GraphQL responses.
7. Reload the storefront and inspect both preflight and actual GraphQL responses.

## Configure each deployment

The plugin contains no deployment-specific URLs. Configure it through
**Settings → GraphQL CORS** in WordPress admin; no server configuration file edits
are required. Only administrators with permission to manage site settings can
save the allowlist. Enter one exact frontend origin per line.

For the current production storefront, add **https://next-woocommerce.dfweb.no**.
Only if this backend should also accept local development requests, add
**http://localhost:3000** (or the actual development port). Do not add the WordPress
backend URL: the allowlist identifies the frontend sites calling it.

Use exact origins, including scheme and port when needed, without paths or
trailing slashes. Multiple origins can be listed if the same backend intentionally
serves several storefronts. Invalid entries are rejected without replacing the
previously saved list. Empty configuration produces an admin warning and grants
no cross-origin access.

### Optional deployment override

For installations managed through server configuration, the existing constant
remains supported. If defined before WordPress loads, it overrides the admin
setting and makes the form read-only:

```php
define( 'HEADLESS_GRAPHQL_ALLOWED_ORIGINS', [
    'https://shop.example.com',
] );
```

Replace the sample origin with the actual frontend origin for that deployment.

For local development, explicitly add `http://localhost:3000` to the development
backend's configuration only if needed. Do not automatically trust local origins
or staging deployments on production backends. Do not reflect arbitrary origins,
allow every subdomain, or replace the allowlist with a wildcard.

Without an override, the plugin uses the allowlist saved in WordPress admin.
An invalid override type fails closed rather than falling back to the saved list.
Each WordPress deployment must configure its own allowlist; setting a Next.js
environment variable does not configure the WordPress server.

## Verify

In browser developer tools, inspect the GraphQL network requests. Both the
preflight and actual responses for the storefront must include:

```text
Access-Control-Allow-Origin: https://shop.example.com
Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true
```

The preflight must allow the POST method and the Content-Type and
woocommerce-session request headers. The actual response must expose the
woocommerce-session response header so Apollo can read the session token.

There must be only one Access-Control-Allow-Origin response header. If a wildcard
remains, inspect nginx, the CDN, caching rules, or another plugin: an upstream
header cannot be removed by changing WPGraphQL's header array alone.

Keep credentials enabled in the frontend: authentication relies on cookies.
CORS is not authentication or CSRF protection. Existing login, authorization, and
CSRF protections must remain in place. If login still fails after CORS succeeds,
inspect cookie domain/path/security settings separately. Different origins may be
same-site or cross-site depending on their domains and schemes. Cross-site cookies
have additional browser restrictions; do not change SameSite blindly.

## Why this hook

WPGraphQL's response-header filter runs for both OPTIONS preflight and regular
GraphQL responses. The plugin uses this supported hook rather than modifying
WPGraphQL core or sending unrelated CORS headers across all WordPress routes.

The plugin is a deployment artifact in this frontend repository. Merely deploying
the Next.js application does not install it on WordPress.
```

  cssClass?: string;

## File: src/components/Animations/FadeLeftToRight.component.tsx
```typescript
import { LazyMotion, domAnimation, m, Transition } from 'motion/react';
import type { IAnimateStaggerWithDelayProps } from './types/Animations.types';
/**
 * Fade content left to right. Needs to be used with FadeLeftToRightItem
 * @function FadeLeftToRight
 * @param {ReactNode} children - Children content to render
 * @param {string} cssClass - CSS classes to apply to component
 * @param {number} delay - Time to wait before starting animation
 * @param {number} staggerDelay - Time to wait before starting animation for children items
 * @param {boolean} animateNotReverse - Start animation backwards
 * @returns {JSX.Element} - Rendered component
 */
const FadeLeftToRight = ({
  children,
  cssClass,
  delay,
  staggerDelay,
  animateNotReverse,
}: IAnimateStaggerWithDelayProps) => {
  const visibleTransition: Transition = {
    when: 'beforeChildren',
    staggerChildren: staggerDelay ? staggerDelay : 0.5,
    delay,
    ease: 'easeInOut',
    staggerDirection: 1,
  };
  const hiddenTransition: Transition = {
    when: 'afterChildren',
    staggerChildren: staggerDelay ? staggerDelay : 0.5,
    staggerDirection: -1,
  };
  const FadeLeftToRightVariants = {
    visible: {
      opacity: 1,
      transition: visibleTransition,
    },
    hidden: {
      opacity: 0,
      transition: hiddenTransition,
    },
  };
  return (
    <LazyMotion features={domAnimation}>
      <m.div
        initial="hidden"
        animate={animateNotReverse ? 'visible' : 'hidden'}
        variants={FadeLeftToRightVariants}
        className={cssClass}
        data-testid="fadelefttoright"
      >
        {children}
      </m.div>
    </LazyMotion>
  );
};
export default FadeLeftToRight;
```

## File: src/components/Animations/FadeLeftToRightItem.component.tsx
```typescript
import { m, Transition } from 'motion/react';
import type { IAnimateProps } from './types/Animations.types';
/**
 * Fade content left to right. Needs to be used with FadeLeftToRight as parent container
 * @function FadeLeftToRightItem
 * @param {ReactNode} children - Children content to render
 * @param {string} cssClass - CSS classes to apply to component
 * @returns {JSX.Element} - Rendered component
 */
const transition: Transition = {
  type: 'spring',
  duration: 0.5,
  stiffness: 110,
};
const fadeLeftToRightItemVariants = {
  visible: {
    opacity: 1,
    x: 0,
    transition,
  },
  hidden: { opacity: 0, x: -20 },
};
const FadeLeftToRightItem = ({ children, cssClass }: IAnimateProps) => {
  return (
    <m.span
      variants={fadeLeftToRightItemVariants}
      className={cssClass}
      data-testid="fadelefttorightitem"
    >
      {children}
    </m.span>
  );
};
export default FadeLeftToRightItem;
