```typescript
/*eslint complexity: ["error", 20]*/
import { v4 as uuidv4 } from 'uuid';
import { ChangeEvent } from 'react';
import { parsePrice } from './price';
import type { CartProduct, Cart } from '@/types/cart';
import type { ICheckoutDataProps } from '@/types/checkout';
import type {
  ICartItemNode,
  IUpdateCartMutationArgs,
  IFormattedCartProps,
} from '@/types/graphql';
// Re-export types that other files import from here
export type { ICartItemNode } from '@/types/graphql';
export type { ICheckoutDataProps } from '@/types/checkout';
export type { IUpdateCartItem } from '@/types/graphql';
export type { IUpdateCartInput } from '@/types/graphql';
export type { IUpdateCartVariables } from '@/types/graphql';
export type { IUpdateCartMutationArgs } from '@/types/graphql';
// Keep backward-compatible alias for IProductRootObject → ICartItemNode
export type IProductRootObject = ICartItemNode;
export type IUpdateCartRootObject = IUpdateCartMutationArgs;
type TUpdatedItems = { key: string; quantity: number }[];
/**
 * Shorten inputted string (usually product description) to a maximum of length
 * @param {string} input The string that we input
 * @param {number} length The length that we want to shorten the text to
 */
export const trimmedStringToLength = (input: string, length: number) => {
  if (input.length > length) {
    const subStr = input.substring(0, length);
    return `${subStr}...`;
  }
  return input;
};
/**
 * Filter variant price. Changes "kr198.00 - kr299.00" to kr299.00 or kr198 depending on the side variable
 * @param {String} side Which side of the string to return (which side of the "-" symbol)
 * @param {String} price The inputted price that we need to convert
 */
export const filteredVariantPrice = (price: string, side: string) => {
  if ('right' === side) {
    return price.substring(price.length, price.indexOf('-')).replace('-', '');
  }
  return price.substring(0, price.indexOf('-')).replace('-', '');
};
/**
 * Returns cart data in the required format.
 * @param {IFormattedCartProps} data Cart data from GraphQL
 */
export const getFormattedCart = (data: IFormattedCartProps) => {
  if (!data?.cart?.contents?.nodes?.length) {
    return;
  }
  const givenProducts = data.cart.contents.nodes;
  const formattedCart: Cart = {
    products: [],
    totalProductsCount: 0,
    totalProductsPrice: 0,
  };
  let totalProductsCount = 0;
  givenProducts.forEach((item) => {
    const givenProduct = item.product.node;
    const convertedCurrency = parsePrice(item.total);
    // Create a new product object for each item to avoid shared reference bug
    const product: CartProduct = {
      productId: givenProduct.productId,
      cartKey: item.key,
      name: givenProduct.name,
      qty: item.quantity,
      price: convertedCurrency / item.quantity,
      totalPrice: item.total,
      image: givenProduct.image?.sourceUrl
        ? {
            sourceUrl: givenProduct.image.sourceUrl,
            srcSet: givenProduct.image.srcSet,
            title: givenProduct.image.title,
          }
        : {
            sourceUrl: process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_PLACEHOLDER_SMALL_IMAGE_URL,
            srcSet: process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_PLACEHOLDER_SMALL_IMAGE_URL,
            title: givenProduct.name,
          },
    };
    totalProductsCount += item.quantity;
    formattedCart.products.push(product);
  });
  formattedCart.totalProductsCount = totalProductsCount;
  formattedCart.totalProductsPrice = parsePrice(data.cart.total);
  return formattedCart;
};
export const createCheckoutData = (order: ICheckoutDataProps) => ({
  clientMutationId: uuidv4(),
  billing: {
    firstName: order.firstName,
    lastName: order.lastName,
    address1: order.address1,
    address2: order.address2,
    city: order.city,
    country: order.country,
    state: order.state,
    postcode: order.postcode,
    email: order.email,
    phone: order.phone,
    company: order.company,
  },
  shipping: {
    firstName: order.firstName,
    lastName: order.lastName,
    address1: order.address1,
    address2: order.address2,
    city: order.city,
    country: order.country,
    state: order.state,
    postcode: order.postcode,
    email: order.email,
    phone: order.phone,
    company: order.company,
  },
  shipToDifferentAddress: false,
  paymentMethod: order.paymentMethod,
  isPaid: false,
  transactionId: uuidv4(),
});
/**
 * Get the updated items in the below format required for mutation input.
 *
 * Creates an array in above format with the newQty (updated Qty ).
 *
 */
export const getUpdatedItems = (
  products: ICartItemNode[],
  newQty: number,
  cartKey: string,
) => {
  const updatedItems: TUpdatedItems = products.map((cartItem) => ({
    key: cartItem.key,
    quantity: cartItem.key === cartKey ? newQty : cartItem.quantity,
  }));
  return updatedItems;
};
/*
 * When user changes the quantity, update the cart in localStorage
 * Also update the cart in the global Context
 */
export const handleQuantityChange = (
  event: ChangeEvent<HTMLInputElement>,
  cartKey: string,
  cart: ICartItemNode[],
  updateCart: (variables: IUpdateCartMutationArgs) => void,
  updateCartProcessing: boolean,
) => {
  if (typeof window !== 'undefined') {
    event.stopPropagation();
    // Return if the previous update cart mutation request is still processing
    if (updateCartProcessing || !cart) {
      return;
    }
    // If the user tries to delete the count of product, set that to 1 by default ( This will not allow him to reduce it less than zero )
    const newQty = event.target.value ? parseInt(event.target.value, 10) : 1;
    if (cart.length) {
      const updatedItems = getUpdatedItems(cart, newQty, cartKey);
      updateCart({
        variables: {
          input: {
            clientMutationId: uuidv4(),
            items: updatedItems,
          },
        },
      });
    }
  }
};
```

## File: src/utils/functions/price.ts
```typescript
type Price = string | number | null | undefined;
// Store-wide display policy: Norwegian kroner, decimal commas and two decimals.
const priceFormatter = new Intl.NumberFormat('nb-NO', {
  minimumFractionDigits: 2,
  maximumFractionDigits: 2,
});
/** Extract price text without rendering HTML or interpreting entity digits as money. */
const getPriceText = (price: string): string => {
  const characters: string[] = [];
  let skipUntil: '>' | ';' | null = null;
  for (const character of price.replace(
    /&(?:ndash|mdash|#8211|#8212);/gi,
    '-',
  )) {
    if (skipUntil) {
      if (character === skipUntil) skipUntil = null;
    } else if (character === '<') {
      skipUntil = '>';
    } else if (character === '&') {
      characters.push(' ');
      skipUntil = ';';
    } else {
      characters.push(character);
    }
  }
  return characters.join('');
};
/** Parse raw or WooCommerce display prices; ranges use the first (lowest) price. */
export const parsePrice = (price: Price): number => {
  if (typeof price === 'number') return price;
  const text = getPriceText(price ?? '');
  const amount = /-?\d[\d\s.,]*/.exec(text)?.[0].replace(/\s/g, '');
  if (!amount) return Number.NaN;
  if (/^-?\d{1,3}([.,]\d{3})+$/.test(amount)) {
    return Number(amount.replace(/[.,]/g, ''));
  }
  const separator = Math.max(amount.lastIndexOf(','), amount.lastIndexOf('.'));
  if (separator === -1) return Number(amount);
  const whole = amount.slice(0, separator).replace(/[.,]/g, '');
  const fraction = amount.slice(separator + 1);
  return Number(`${whole}.${fraction}`);
};
/** The single display formatter for all prices, including variable-product ranges. */
export const formatPrice = (price: Price): string => {
  if (typeof price === 'string') {
    const range = getPriceText(price).split(/(?<=\d)\s*[-–—]\s*/);
    if (range.length > 1)
      return range.map((part) => formatPrice(parsePrice(part))).join(' - ');
  }
  const amount = parsePrice(price);
  return Number.isFinite(amount)
    ? `kr ${priceFormatter.format(amount).replace(/\s/g, ' ')}`
    : 'N/A';
};
/** Calculate a unit price from a formatted line subtotal using the same display policy. */
export const formatUnitPrice = (subtotal: Price, quantity: number): string =>
  formatPrice(
    Number.isFinite(quantity) && quantity > 0
      ? parsePrice(subtotal) / quantity
      : Number.NaN,
  );
```

## File: src/utils/functions/productUtils.ts
```typescript
import { Product, ProductCategory, ProductType } from '@/types/product';
import { parsePrice } from './price';
// Preserve the existing public name while sharing the store-wide price parser.
export { parsePrice as parseProductPrice } from './price';
export const getUniqueProductTypes = (products: Product[]): ProductType[] => {
  // Use Map to ensure unique categories by slug
  const categoryMap = new Map<string, ProductType>();
  products?.forEach((product) => {
    product.productCategories?.nodes.forEach((cat: ProductCategory) => {
      if (!categoryMap.has(cat.slug)) {
        categoryMap.set(cat.slug, {
          id: cat.slug,
          name: cat.name,
          checked: false,
        });
      }
    });
  });
  // Convert Map values to array and sort by name
  return Array.from(categoryMap.values()).sort((a, b) =>
    a.name.localeCompare(b.name),
  );
};
/** Keep the slider usable for small catalogs without excluding expensive items. */
export const getMaxProductPrice = (products: Product[]): number =>
  products.reduce((max, product) => {
    const price = parsePrice(product.price);
    return Number.isFinite(price) ? Math.max(max, Math.ceil(price)) : max;
  }, 1000);
/** Check if a product's price falls within the given range */
export const isWithinPriceRange = (
  product: Product,
  priceRange: [number, number],
): boolean => {
  const productPrice = parsePrice(product.price);
  return productPrice >= priceRange[0] && productPrice <= priceRange[1];
};
/** Check if a product matches any of the selected product types (categories) */
export const matchesProductType = (
  product: Product,
  productTypes: ProductType[],
): boolean => {
  const selectedTypes = productTypes.reduce<string[]>((acc, type) => {
    if (type.checked) {
      acc.push(type.name.toLowerCase());
    }
    return acc;
  }, []);
  if (selectedTypes.length === 0) return true;
  const productCategorySet = new Set(
    product.productCategories?.nodes.map((cat) => cat.name.toLowerCase()) || [],
  );
  return selectedTypes.some((type) => productCategorySet.has(type));
};
/** Check if a product matches any of the selected sizes */
export const matchesSize = (
  product: Product,
  selectedSizes: string[],
): boolean => {
  if (selectedSizes.length === 0) return true;
  const productSizeSet = new Set(
    product.allPaSizes?.nodes.map((node) => node.name) || [],
  );
  return selectedSizes.some((size) => productSizeSet.has(size));
};
/** Check if a product matches any of the selected colors */
export const matchesColor = (
  product: Product,
  selectedColors: string[],
): boolean => {
  if (selectedColors.length === 0) return true;
  const productColorSet = new Set(
    product.allPaColors?.nodes.map((node) => node.name) || [],
  );
  return selectedColors.some((color) => productColorSet.has(color));
};
/** Sort comparator for products based on sort criteria */
export const getProductSortComparator = (
  sortBy: string,
): ((a: Product, b: Product) => number) => {
  switch (sortBy) {
    case 'price-low':
      return (a, b) => parsePrice(a.price) - parsePrice(b.price);
    case 'price-high':
      return (a, b) => parsePrice(b.price) - parsePrice(a.price);
    case 'newest':
      return (a, b) => b.databaseId - a.databaseId;
    default:
      return () => 0;
  }
};
```

## File: src/utils/auth.ts
```typescript
import { ApolloClient, InMemoryCache } from '@apollo/client';
import { LOGIN_USER } from './gql/GQL_MUTATIONS';
type LoginError = {
  graphQLErrors?: Array<{ message?: string }>;
  networkError?: unknown;
  message?: string;
};
const GRAPHQL_ERROR_MESSAGES: Record<string, string> = {
  invalid_username:
    'Ugyldig brukernavn eller e-postadresse. Vennligst sjekk og prøv igjen.',
  incorrect_password:
    'Feil passord. Vennligst sjekk passordet ditt og prøv igjen.',
  invalid_email:
    'Ugyldig e-postadresse. Vennligst skriv inn en gyldig e-postadresse.',
  empty_username: 'Vennligst skriv inn brukernavn eller e-postadresse.',
  empty_password: 'Vennligst skriv inn passord.',
  too_many_retries:
    'For mange mislykkede forsøk. Vennligst vent litt før du prøver igjen.',
};
const DEFAULT_GRAPHQL_ERROR =
  'Innlogging mislyktes. Vennligst sjekk dine opplysninger og prøv igjen.';
const NETWORK_ERROR =
  'Nettverksfeil. Vennligst sjekk internetttilkoblingen din og prøv igjen.';
const GENERIC_LOGIN_ERROR =
  'Det oppstod en feil under innlogging. Vennligst prøv igjen.';
const UNKNOWN_ERROR =
  'En ukjent feil oppstod. Vennligst prøv igjen senere.';
function getErrorMessage(error: unknown): string {
  if (typeof error !== 'object' || error === null) {
    return UNKNOWN_ERROR;
  }
  const loginError = error as LoginError;
  // Check for GraphQL errors
  if (loginError.graphQLErrors && loginError.graphQLErrors.length > 0) {
    const message = loginError.graphQLErrors[0].message ?? '';
    return GRAPHQL_ERROR_MESSAGES[message] ?? DEFAULT_GRAPHQL_ERROR;
  }
  // Check for network errors
  if (loginError.networkError) {
    return NETWORK_ERROR;
  }
  // Fallback for other errors
  if (loginError.message) {
    return GENERIC_LOGIN_ERROR;
  }
  return UNKNOWN_ERROR;
}
export async function login(username: string, password: string) {
  try {
    const client = new ApolloClient({
      uri: process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_GRAPHQL_URL,
      cache: new InMemoryCache(),
      credentials: 'include', // Include cookies in requests
    });
    const { data } = await client.mutate({
      mutation: LOGIN_USER,
      variables: { username, password },
    });
    const loginResult = data.loginWithCookies;
    if (loginResult.status !== 'SUCCESS') {
      throw new Error(
        'Innlogging mislyktes. Vennligst sjekk dine opplysninger og prøv igjen.',
      );
    }
    // On successful login, cookies are automatically set by the server
    return { success: true, status: loginResult.status };
  } catch (error: unknown) {
    const userFriendlyMessage = getErrorMessage(error);
    throw new Error(userFriendlyMessage);
  }
}
/*
export async function logout() {
  // For cookie-based auth, we might need a logout mutation
  // For now, we can clear any client-side state
  if (typeof window !== 'undefined') {
    // Redirect to login or home page after logout
    window.location.href = '/';
  }
}
*/

## File: TODO/UserRegistration.component.txt

// ============================================================================
// USER REGISTRATION - READY-TO-USE REFERENCE
// ============================================================================
//
// STATUS: Parked. The frontend code below is complete and verified against
// the backend's schema. It is NOT wired into the app yet because registration
// depends on a WordPress server setting (see PREREQUISITE).
//
// PREREQUISITE (backend, one-time):
//   WordPress admin -> Settings -> General -> Membership ->
//   check "Anyone can register".
//   Until this is enabled, the backend rejects every attempt with:
//     "User registration is currently not allowed."
//
// WHY registerCustomer (NOT registerUser):
//   For a WooCommerce store we want accounts with the `customer` role (plus
//   billing/shipping support), NOT plain WordPress subscribers. Verified live
//   against the backend on 2026-10-14:
//     - registerUser  -> creates a user with role "subscriber" (wrong for a
//       shop; it uses WordPress's default new-user role).
//     - registerCustomer -> creates a proper WooCommerce customer; the new
//       account logs in successfully via loginWithCookies (status SUCCESS).
//   Both mutations are exposed; registerCustomer is the correct one here.
//
// TO ACTIVATE (once "Anyone can register" is on):
//   1. Add REGISTER_CUSTOMER to src/utils/gql/GQL_MUTATIONS.ts (see block below).
//   2. Save the component block below as
//      src/components/User/UserRegistration.component.tsx.
//   3. Rename src/pages/registrer.tsx.backup -> src/pages/registrer.tsx.
//   4. (Optional) Add a "Registrer deg" link to UserLogin.component.tsx.
//
// ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 1) GraphQL mutation -> src/utils/gql/GQL_MUTATIONS.ts
// ----------------------------------------------------------------------------

export const REGISTER_CUSTOMER = gql`
  mutation RegisterCustomer(
    $username: String!
    $email: String!
    $password: String!
    $firstName: String
    $lastName: String
  ) {
    registerCustomer(
      input: {
        username: $username
        email: $email
        password: $password
        firstName: $firstName
        lastName: $lastName
      }
    ) {
      customer {
        id
        email
        firstName
        lastName
      }
    }
  }
`;

// ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 2) Component -> src/components/User/UserRegistration.component.tsx
// ----------------------------------------------------------------------------

import { useState } from 'react';
import Link from 'next/link';
import { useRouter } from 'next/router';
import { useMutation } from '@apollo/client';
import { useForm, FormProvider } from 'react-hook-form';

import { REGISTER_CUSTOMER } from '@/utils/gql/GQL_MUTATIONS';
import { InputField } from '../Input/InputField.component';
import LoadingSpinner from '../LoadingSpinner/LoadingSpinner.component';
import Button from '../UI/Button.component';

interface IRegistrationData {
  username: string;
  email: string;
  password: string;
  firstName: string;
  lastName: string;
}

// Maps backend GraphQL error messages to Norwegian user-facing text.
// The exact strings come from WPGraphQL / WordPress core.
const getRegisterErrorMessage = (message?: string): string => {
  switch (message) {
    case 'User registration is currently not allowed.':
      // "Anyone can register" is disabled in WordPress settings.
      return 'Registrering er for øyeblikket ikke tilgjengelig.';
    case 'existing_user_login':
      return 'Dette brukernavnet er allerede i bruk.';
    case 'existing_user_email':
      return 'Det finnes allerede en konto med denne e-postadressen.';
    case 'empty_username':
      return 'Vennligst skriv inn et brukernavn.';
    case 'empty_email':
    case 'invalid_email':
      return 'Vennligst skriv inn en gyldig e-postadresse.';
    default:
      return 'Registrering mislyktes. Vennligst prøv igjen.';
  }
};

/**
 * User registration component. Creates a WooCommerce customer account (role
 * "customer") via the `registerCustomer` mutation, then redirects to login.
 * @function UserRegistration
 * @returns {JSX.Element} - Rendered component with registration form
 */
const UserRegistration = () => {
  const methods = useForm<IRegistrationData>();
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const { push } = useRouter();

  const [registerCustomer, { loading }] = useMutation(REGISTER_CUSTOMER, {
    onCompleted: () => {
      push('/logg-inn');
    },
    onError: (mutationError) => {
      setError(
        getRegisterErrorMessage(mutationError.graphQLErrors?.[0]?.message),
      );
    },
  });

  const onSubmit = (data: IRegistrationData) => {
    setError(null);
    registerCustomer({ variables: data });
  };

  return (
    <section className="text-text-muted container p-4 py-2 mx-auto mb-32 md:mb-0">
      <FormProvider {...methods}>
        <form onSubmit={methods.handleSubmit(onSubmit)}>
          <div className="mx-auto lg:w-1/2 flex flex-wrap">
            <InputField
              inputName="username"
              inputLabel="Brukernavn"
              type="text"
              customValidation={{ required: true }}
            />
            <InputField
              inputName="email"
              inputLabel="E-post"
              type="email"
              customValidation={{ required: true }}
            />
            <InputField
              inputName="password"
              inputLabel="Passord"
              type="password"
              customValidation={{ required: true }}
            />
            <InputField
              inputName="firstName"
              inputLabel="Fornavn"
              type="text"
              customValidation={{ required: true }}
            />
            <InputField
              inputName="lastName"
              inputLabel="Etternavn"
              type="text"
              customValidation={{ required: true }}
            />

            {error && (
              <div
                className="w-full p-2 text-error text-sm text-center"
                role="alert"
              >
                {error}
              </div>
            )}

            <div className="w-full p-2">
              <div className="mt-4 flex justify-center">
                <Button type="submit" variant="primary" buttonDisabled={loading}>
                  {loading ? <LoadingSpinner /> : 'Registrer'}
                </Button>
              </div>
            </div>

            <div className="w-full p-2 text-center text-sm">
              Har du allerede en konto?{' '}
              <Link href="/logg-inn" className="text-primary hover:underline">
                Logg inn
              </Link>
            </div>
          </div>
        </form>
      </FormProvider>
    </section>
  );
};

export default UserRegistration;
```

## File: wordpress/mu-plugins/headless-graphql-cors.php
```php
<?php
/**
 * Plugin Name: Headless GraphQL CORS
 * Description: Allow credentialed GraphQL requests from the storefront only.
 */
defined( 'ABSPATH' ) || exit;
/** Return deployment overrides, or the origins saved in WordPress admin. */
function headlessGraphqlCorsAllowedOrigins(): array {
    $origins = defined( 'HEADLESS_GRAPHQL_ALLOWED_ORIGINS' )
        ? HEADLESS_GRAPHQL_ALLOWED_ORIGINS
        : get_option( 'headless_graphql_cors_allowed_origins', [] );
    return is_array( $origins ) ? $origins : [];
}
/** Check exact HTTP(S) origin syntax and the parsed port range. */
function headlessGraphqlCorsIsValidOrigin( string $origin, $parts ): bool {
    return preg_match( '#\Ahttps?://(?:[a-z\d](?:[a-z\d.-]*[a-z\d])?|\[[\da-f:]+\])(?::\d{1,5})?\z#', $origin )
        && false !== $parts
        && ( ! isset( $parts['port'] ) || ( $parts['port'] >= 1 && $parts['port'] <= 65535 ) );
}
/** Browsers omit default ports from the Origin header. */
function headlessGraphqlCorsNormalizeOrigin( string $origin, array $parts ): string {
    if ( isset( $parts['port'] ) && ( ( 'https' === $parts['scheme'] && 443 === $parts['port'] ) || ( 'http' === $parts['scheme'] && 80 === $parts['port'] ) ) ) {
        return $parts['scheme'] . '://' . $parts['host'];
    }
    return $origin;
}
/** Reject malformed entries without replacing the previously saved allowlist. */
function headlessGraphqlCorsSanitizeOrigins( $input ): array {
    $previous = get_option( 'headless_graphql_cors_allowed_origins', [] );
    $previous = is_array( $previous ) ? $previous : [];
    // A deployment override is authoritative; do not accept misleading edits.
    if ( defined( 'HEADLESS_GRAPHQL_ALLOWED_ORIGINS' ) ) {
        return $previous;
    }
    // WordPress can sanitize twice when creating an option for the first time.
    $lines = is_string( $input ) ? preg_split( '/\r\n|\r|\n/', $input ) : $input;
    if ( ! is_array( $lines ) ) {
        add_settings_error( 'headless_graphql_cors_allowed_origins', 'invalid_origins', 'Enter one HTTP or HTTPS origin per line.' );
        return $previous;
    }
    return headlessGraphqlCorsSanitizeOriginLines( $lines, $previous );
}
/** Validate every line before replacing the saved allowlist. */
function headlessGraphqlCorsSanitizeOriginLines( array $lines, array $previous ): array {
    $origins = [];
    foreach ( $lines as $line ) {
        if ( ! is_string( $line ) ) {
            add_settings_error( 'headless_graphql_cors_allowed_origins', 'invalid_origins', 'Enter one HTTP or HTTPS origin per line.' );
            return $previous;
        }
        $origin = strtolower( trim( $line ) );
        if ( '' === $origin ) {
            continue;
        }
        $parts = wp_parse_url( $origin );
        if ( ! headlessGraphqlCorsIsValidOrigin( $origin, $parts ) ) {
            add_settings_error(
                'headless_graphql_cors_allowed_origins',
                'invalid_origins',
                'Not saved. Use exact HTTP or HTTPS origins, without paths, trailing slashes, credentials, queries, fragments, or wildcards.'
            );
            return $previous;
        }
        $origins[] = headlessGraphqlCorsNormalizeOrigin( $origin, $parts );
    }
    return array_values( array_unique( $origins ) );
}
add_action(
    'admin_init',
    static function (): void {
        register_setting(
            'headless_graphql_cors',
            'headless_graphql_cors_allowed_origins',
            [
                'type'              => 'array',
                'default'           => [],
                'sanitize_callback' => 'headlessGraphqlCorsSanitizeOrigins',
                'show_in_rest'      => false,
            ]
        );
    }
);
add_action(
    'admin_menu',
    static function (): void {
        add_options_page(
            'GraphQL CORS',
            'GraphQL CORS',
            'manage_options',
            'headless-graphql-cors',
            static function (): void {
                if ( ! current_user_can( 'manage_options' ) ) {
                    return;
                }
                $overridden = defined( 'HEADLESS_GRAPHQL_ALLOWED_ORIGINS' );
                echo '<div class="wrap"><h1>GraphQL CORS</h1>';
                settings_errors( 'headless_graphql_cors_allowed_origins' );
                echo '<p>Allow credentialed GraphQL requests only from storefronts you trust. Enter one exact origin per line.</p>';
                if ( $overridden ) {
                    echo '<p><strong>A deployment configuration override is active. This form is read-only.</strong></p>';
                }
                echo '<form action="options.php" method="post">';
                settings_fields( 'headless_graphql_cors' );
                echo '<p><label for="headless-graphql-cors-origins">Allowed storefront origins</label></p>';
                echo '<textarea id="headless-graphql-cors-origins" name="headless_graphql_cors_allowed_origins" rows="8" cols="70" class="large-text code"' . ( $overridden ? ' disabled' : '' ) . '>';
                echo esc_textarea( implode( "\n", array_filter( headlessGraphqlCorsAllowedOrigins(), 'is_string' ) ) );
                echo '</textarea><p class="description">Example: https://shop.example.com — include the scheme and any non-default port, but no path or trailing slash. An empty list blocks all cross-origin storefront access.</p>';
                if ( ! $overridden ) {
                    submit_button();
                }
                echo '</form></div>';
            }
        );
    }
);
add_action(
    'admin_notices',
    static function (): void {
        if ( ! current_user_can( 'manage_options' ) || ! class_exists( '\WPGraphQL' ) || ! empty( headlessGraphqlCorsAllowedOrigins() ) ) {
            return;
        }
        echo '<div class="notice notice-warning"><p>';
        echo esc_html__( 'Headless GraphQL CORS has no allowed storefront origins. Cross-origin storefront requests will be blocked. Configure Settings → GraphQL CORS.', 'headless-graphql-cors' );
        echo ' <a href="' . esc_url( admin_url( 'options-general.php?page=headless-graphql-cors' ) ) . '">Configure GraphQL CORS</a>';
        echo '</p></div>';
    }
);
// MU plugins load before regular plugins, so defer the dependency check.
add_action(
    'plugins_loaded',
    static function (): void {
        if ( class_exists( '\WPGraphQL' ) ) {
            return;
        }
        add_action(
            'admin_notices',
            static function (): void {
                if ( ! current_user_can( 'manage_options' ) ) {
                    return;
                }
                echo '<div class="notice notice-warning"><p>';
                echo esc_html__(
                    'Headless GraphQL CORS requires WPGraphQL to be installed and active. No GraphQL CORS headers are being configured.',
                    'headless-graphql-cors'
                );
                echo '</p></div>';
            }
        );
    }
);
add_filter(
    'graphql_response_headers_to_send',
    static function ( array $headers ): array {
        // Configure Settings → GraphQL CORS; no origins are trusted by default.
        $allowed_origins = headlessGraphqlCorsAllowedOrigins();
        $origin = get_http_origin();
        // Replace WPGraphQL's wildcard, rather than adding a second origin header.
        unset( $headers['Access-Control-Allow-Origin'], $headers['Access-Control-Allow-Credentials'] );
        // Preserve WPGraphQL's existing cache variation (e.g. X-GraphQL-Preview).
        $headers['Vary'] = empty( $headers['Vary'] ) ? 'Origin' : $headers['Vary'] . ', Origin';
        if ( ! in_array( $origin, $allowed_origins, true ) ) {
            return $headers;
        }
        $headers['Access-Control-Allow-Origin']      = $origin;
        $headers['Access-Control-Allow-Credentials'] = 'true';
        $headers['Access-Control-Allow-Methods']     = 'GET, POST, OPTIONS';
        // Keep headers registered by WPGraphQL/extensions and add storefront needs.
        $allowed_headers = array_map( 'trim', explode( ',', $headers['Access-Control-Allow-Headers'] ?? '' ) );
        $headers['Access-Control-Allow-Headers'] = implode(
            ', ',
            array_unique( array_filter( array_merge( $allowed_headers, [ 'Content-Type', 'Authorization', 'woocommerce-session', 'X-WP-Nonce' ] ) ) )
        );
        $exposed_headers = array_map( 'trim', explode( ',', $headers['Access-Control-Expose-Headers'] ?? '' ) );
        $headers['Access-Control-Expose-Headers'] = implode(
            ', ',
            array_unique( array_filter( array_merge( $exposed_headers, [ 'woocommerce-session' ] ) ) )
        );
        return $headers;
    },
    1000
);
```

## File: algolia-replace-with-wpgraphql.txt
```
▐▛███▛█   Claude Code v2.1.309
▝▜██████▀  Sonnet 5.5 · Claude Team
 ▝▝   ▝▝   C:\dev\wordpress\nextjs-woocom


❯ this project is Next.js (React) headless eCommerce site with Typescript,      
  WordPress (WooCommerce) backend and Algolia search... if i wanna remove the   
  dependency of algolia search from this to connect with woocommerce... what    
  have to do for this project?                                                  

● I'll start by exploring how Algolia is wired into the project.

● Explore(Explore Algolia usage)
  ⎿  Backgrounded agent (↓ to manage · ctrl+o to expand)

● The Explore agent is still mapping where Algolia is used. I'll wait for its
  report before planning.

✻ Brewed for 7s · done 11:14 PM

● Agent "Explore Algolia usage" finished · 33s

● Updated plan
  ⎿  /plan to preview
  ⎿  User rejected Claude's plan:
     ╭─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
     │ Plan: Replace Algolia search with WooCommerce (WPGraphQL) search        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Context                                                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Search currently uses Algolia (algoliasearch/lite +                     │
     │ react-instantsearch) via an external WP indexer plugin                  │
     │ (wp-algolia-woo-indexer). The rest of the site already talks to         │
     │ WooCommerce through WPGraphQL + Apollo. Algolia is used only for the    │
     │ header/mobile/sticky-nav search box dropdown (no results page, no       │
     │ facets, no indexing code in the repo), so removal is small and          │
     │ self-contained. Goal: search products live from WooCommerce, with no    │
     │ Algolia dependency, keys or plugin.                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Approach                                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Use WPGraphQL's built-in product search: products(first: N, where: {    │
     │ search: $term }). No WordPress changes are needed (WooGraphQL already   │
     │ provides it). Search is MySQL LIKE-based, so it is slower and less      │
     │ typo-tolerant than Algolia; mitigate with debounce, min 2 chars, and a  │
     │ small first (e.g. 8).                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Steps                                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Add query SEARCH_PRODUCTS_QUERY to src/utils/gql/GQL_QUERIES.ts. It  │
     │    takes $search: String! and $first: Int, and returns databaseId,      │
     │    name, slug, shortDescription, onSale, image{sourceUrl}, plus price,  │
     │    regularPrice, salePrice on the simple/variable product fragments     │
     │    (match what FETCH_ALL_PRODUCTS_QUERY already selects).               │
     │ 2. Add hook src/hooks/useProductSearch.ts:                              │
     │    - Debounces the input (~300 ms), ignores terms shorter than 2        │
     │      characters.                                                        │
     │    - Uses Apollo useLazyQuery (or useQuery with skip) with the existing │
     │      client in src/utils/apollo/ApolloClient.ts.                        │
     │    - Returns { query, setQuery, results, loading }.                     │
     │ 3. Rewrite the UI in src/components/AlgoliaSearch/. Rename the folder   │
     │    to ProductSearch/, update imports in Header/Navbar.component.tsx and │
     │    Footer/Stickynav.component.tsx:                                      │
     │    - AlgoliaSearchBox → SearchBox: plain controlled <input> plus        │
     │      dropdown list, using useProductSearch. Remove <InstantSearch>,     │
     │      liteClient, useSearchBox and <Hits>.                               │
     │    - MobileSearch: same hook, inline results. Share one component with  │
     │      a variant prop to remove the duplicated code.                      │
     │    - SearchResults → SearchResultItem: takes GraphQL product fields     │
     │      (name, image.sourceUrl, price/salePrice/regularPrice,              │
     │      shortDescription). Keep formatPrice and trimmedStringToLength.     │
     │      Strip HTML from shortDescription.                                  │
     │    - Fix link bug: link to /produkt/${slug}, not the name with spaces   │
     │      replaced (this was fragile with Algolia).                          │
     │    - Keep the Norwegian labels and the existing layout classes.         │
     │ 4. Optional: add a /sok?q= results page that reuses ProductCard (the    │

● User approved Claude's plan
  ⎿  Plan saved to: ~\.claude\plans\this-project-is-next-js-joyful-sundae.md · 
     /plan to edit
     Plan: Replace Algolia search with WooCommerce (WPGraphQL) search

     Context

     Search currently uses Algolia (algoliasearch/lite + react-instantsearch)
     via an external WP indexer plugin (wp-algolia-woo-indexer). The rest of the
     site already talks to WooCommerce through WPGraphQL + Apollo. Algolia is
     used only for the header/mobile/sticky-nav search box dropdown (no results
     page, no facets, no indexing code in the repo), so removal is small and
     self-contained. Goal: search products live from WooCommerce, with no
     Algolia dependency, keys or plugin.

     Approach

     Use WPGraphQL's built-in product search: products(first: N, where: { 
     search: $term }). No WordPress changes are needed (WooGraphQL already
     provides it). Search is MySQL LIKE-based, so it is slower and less
     typo-tolerant than Algolia; mitigate with debounce, min 2 chars, and a
     small first (e.g. 8).

     Steps

     1. Add query SEARCH_PRODUCTS_QUERY to src/utils/gql/GQL_QUERIES.ts. It
        takes $search: String! and $first: Int, and returns databaseId, name, 
        slug, shortDescription, onSale, image{sourceUrl}, plus price, 
        regularPrice, salePrice on the simple/variable product fragments (match
        what FETCH_ALL_PRODUCTS_QUERY already selects).
     2. Add hook src/hooks/useProductSearch.ts:
        - Debounces the input (~300 ms), ignores terms shorter than 2
          characters.
        - Uses Apollo useLazyQuery (or useQuery with skip) with the existing
          client in src/utils/apollo/ApolloClient.ts.
        - Returns { query, setQuery, results, loading }.
     3. Rewrite the UI in src/components/AlgoliaSearch/. Rename the folder to
        ProductSearch/, update imports in Header/Navbar.component.tsx and
        Footer/Stickynav.component.tsx:
        - AlgoliaSearchBox → SearchBox: plain controlled <input> plus dropdown
          list, using useProductSearch. Remove <InstantSearch>, liteClient,
          useSearchBox and <Hits>.
        - MobileSearch: same hook, inline results. Share one component with a
          variant prop to remove the duplicated code.
        - SearchResults → SearchResultItem: takes GraphQL product fields (name,
          image.sourceUrl, price/salePrice/regularPrice, shortDescription). Keep
          formatPrice and trimmedStringToLength. Strip HTML from
          shortDescription.
        - Fix link bug: link to /produkt/${slug}, not the name with spaces
          replaced (this was fragile with Algolia).
        - Keep the Norwegian labels and the existing layout classes.
     4. Optional: add a /sok?q= results page that reuses ProductCard (the
        GraphQL product shape already matches it, so this would be easy). Skip
        unless wanted.
     5. Remove Algolia:
        - npm uninstall algoliasearch react-instantsearch.
        - Delete src/styles/algolia.min.css and its import. Remove the "Fix
          Algolia mobile searchbox" block in src/styles/globals.css (~line 128),
          keeping any rules the new input still needs.
        - Remove the three NEXT_PUBLIC_ALGOLIA_* vars from .env.example and from
          deployment env.
        - Delete plans/algolia-upgrade-plan.md. Update README.md, TODO.md,
          DOCS/repository_context.txt.
     6. WordPress side (outside the repo): deactivate and remove the
        wp-algolia-woo-indexer plugin and revoke the Algolia keys. Optionally
        install a search-relevance plugin (e.g. SearchWP, Relevanssi, or
        ElasticPress/Elasticsearch/Meilisearch) if default WP search quality is
        not enough. WPGraphQL where.search will then use it automatically.

     Critical files

     - src/utils/gql/GQL_QUERIES.ts (add query)
     - src/hooks/useProductSearch.ts (new)
     - src/components/AlgoliaSearch/* → src/components/ProductSearch/*
     - src/components/Header/Navbar.component.tsx,
       src/components/Footer/Stickynav.component.tsx (imports)
     - package.json, .env.example, src/styles/globals.css,
       src/styles/algolia.min.css

     Verification

     - npm run build and tsc --noEmit pass; grep -ri algolia src package.json 
       .env.example returns nothing.
     - npm run dev: type in desktop, mobile and sticky-nav search; confirm
       debounce (one request per pause), a results dropdown, and click-through
       to the correct /produkt/<slug> page.
     - Check empty results, a single-character query, a product with no image,
       and sale-price rendering.
     - Confirm in the network tab that no requests go to algolia.net.
     - Compare search quality (partial words, typos, Norwegian characters)
       against Algolia and decide whether a relevance plugin is needed.

● Background command "cat >> src/utils/gql/GQL_QUERIES.ts <<'EOF' export const
SEARCH_PRODUCTS_QUERY = gql` query SearchProducts($search: String!, $first: Int
= 8) { products(first: $first, where: { search: $search }) { nodes { databaseId
name slug onSale shortDescription(format: RAW) image { sourceUrl } ... on
SimpleProduct { price regularPrice salePrice } ... on VariableProduct { price
regularPrice salePrice } } } } `; EOF cat > src/hooks/useProductSearch.ts
<<'EOF' import { useEffect, useState } from 'react'; import { useQuery } from
'@apollo/client'; import { SEARCH_PRODUCTS_QUERY } from
'@/utils/gql/GQL_QUERIES'; export interface ISearchProduct { databaseId: number;
name: string; slug: string; onSale: boolean; shortDescription?: string | null;
image?: { sourceUrl?: string | null } | null; price?: string | null;
regularPrice?: string | null; salePrice?: string | null; } const DEBOUNCE_MS =
300; const MIN_LENGTH = 2; /** * Debounced WooCommerce product search through
WPGraphQL. */ export const useProductSearch = () => { const [query, setQuery] =
useState(''); const [debounced, setDebounced] = useState(''); useEffect(() => {
const timer = setTimeout(() => setDebounced(query.trim()), DEBOUNCE_MS); return
() => clearTimeout(timer); }, [query]); const active = debounced.length >=
MIN_LENGTH && query.trim().length >= MIN_LENGTH; const { data, loading } =
useQuery<{ products: { nodes: ISearchProduct[] } }>( SEARCH_PRODUCTS_QUERY, {
variables: { search: debounced }, skip: !active }, ); return { query, setQuery,
active, loading, results: active ? (data?.products?.nodes ?? []) : [], }; }; EOF

produkter" value={query} onChange={(e) => setQuery(e.target.value)} onFocus={()
=> setHasFocus(true)} onBlur={() => setHasFocus(false)} className={`${isDesktop

# Contributor Covenant Code of Conduct

## Our Pledge

In the interest of fostering an open and welcoming environment, we as
contributors and maintainers pledge to making participation in our project and
our community a harassment-free experience for everyone, regardless of age, body
size, disability, ethnicity, sex characteristics, gender identity and expression,
level of experience, education, socio-economic status, nationality, personal
appearance, race, religion, or sexual identity and orientation.

## Our Standards

Examples of behavior that contributes to creating a positive environment
include:

-   Using welcoming and inclusive language
-   Being respectful of differing viewpoints and experiences
-   Gracefully accepting constructive criticism
-   Focusing on what is best for the community
-   Showing empathy towards other community members

Examples of unacceptable behavior by participants include:

-   The use of sexualized language or imagery and unwelcome sexual attention or
    advances
-   Trolling, insulting/derogatory comments, and personal or political attacks
-   Public or private harassment
-   Publishing others' private information, such as a physical or electronic
    address, without explicit permission
-   Other conduct which could reasonably be considered inappropriate in a
    professional setting

## Our Responsibilities

Project maintainers are responsible for clarifying the standards of acceptable
behavior and are expected to take appropriate and fair corrective action in
response to any instances of unacceptable behavior.

Project maintainers have the right and responsibility to remove, edit, or
reject comments, commits, code, wiki edits, issues, and other contributions
that are not aligned to this Code of Conduct, or to ban temporarily or
permanently any contributor for other behaviors that they deem inappropriate,
threatening, offensive, or harmful.

## Scope

This Code of Conduct applies both within project spaces and in public spaces
when an individual is representing the project or its community. Examples of
representing a project or community include using an official project e-mail
address, posting via an official social media account, or acting as an appointed
representative at an online or offline event. Representation of a project may be
further defined and clarified by project maintainers.

## Enforcement

Instances of abusive, harassing, or otherwise unacceptable behavior may be
reported by contacting the project team at . All
complaints will be reviewed and investigated and will result in a response that
is deemed necessary and appropriate to the circumstances. The project team is
obligated to maintain confidentiality with regard to the reporter of an incident.
Further details of specific enforcement policies may be posted separately.

Project maintainers who do not follow or enforce the Code of Conduct in good
faith may face temporary or permanent repercussions as determined by other
members of the project's leadership.

## Attribution

This Code of Conduct is adapted from the [Contributor Covenant][homepage], version 1.4,
available at <https://www.contributor-covenant.org/version/1/4/code-of-conduct.html>

[homepage]: https://www.contributor-covenant.org

For answers to common questions about this code of conduct, see
<https://www.contributor-covenant.org/faq>
```

## File: CONTRIBUTING.md
```markdown
# Contributing

When contributing to this repository, please first discuss the change you wish to make via issue,
email, or any other method with the owners of this repository before making a change. 

Please note we have a code of conduct, please follow it in all your interactions with the project.

## Pull Request Process

1.  Ensure any install or build dependencies are removed before the end of the layer when doing a 
    build.
2.  Update the README.md with details of changes to the interface, this includes new environment 
    variables, exposed ports, useful file locations and container parameters.
3.  Increase the version numbers in any examples files and the README.md to the new version that this
    Pull Request would represent. The versioning scheme we use is [SemVer](http://semver.org/).
4.  You may merge the Pull Request in once you have the sign-off of two other developers, or if you 
    do not have permission to do that, you may request the second reviewer to merge it for you.

## Code of Conduct

### Our Pledge

In the interest of fostering an open and welcoming environment, we as
contributors and maintainers pledge to making participation in our project and
our community a harassment-free experience for everyone, regardless of age, body
size, disability, ethnicity, gender identity and expression, level of experience,
nationality, personal appearance, race, religion, or sexual identity and
orientation.

### Our Standards

Examples of behavior that contributes to creating a positive environment
include:

-   Using welcoming and inclusive language
-   Being respectful of differing viewpoints and experiences
-   Gracefully accepting constructive criticism
-   Focusing on what is best for the community
-   Showing empathy towards other community members

Examples of unacceptable behavior by participants include:

-   The use of sexualized language or imagery and unwelcome sexual attention or
    advances
-   Trolling, insulting/derogatory comments, and personal or political attacks
-   Public or private harassment
-   Publishing others' private information, such as a physical or electronic
    address, without explicit permission
-   Other conduct which could reasonably be considered inappropriate in a
    professional setting

### Our Responsibilities

Project maintainers are responsible for clarifying the standards of acceptable
behavior and are expected to take appropriate and fair corrective action in
response to any instances of unacceptable behavior.

Project maintainers have the right and responsibility to remove, edit, or
reject comments, commits, code, wiki edits, issues, and other contributions
that are not aligned to this Code of Conduct, or to ban temporarily or
permanently any contributor for other behaviors that they deem inappropriate,
threatening, offensive, or harmful.

### Scope

This Code of Conduct applies both within project spaces and in public spaces
when an individual is representing the project or its community. Examples of
representing a project or community include using an official project e-mail
address, posting via an official social media account, or acting as an appointed
representative at an online or offline event. Representation of a project may be
further defined and clarified by project maintainers.

### Enforcement

Instances of abusive, harassing, or otherwise unacceptable behavior may be
reported by contacting the project team at [INSERT EMAIL ADDRESS]. All
complaints will be reviewed and investigated and will result in a response that
is deemed necessary and appropriate to the circumstances. The project team is
obligated to maintain confidentiality with regard to the reporter of an incident.
Further details of specific enforcement policies may be posted separately.

Project maintainers who do not follow or enforce the Code of Conduct in good
faith may face temporary or permanent repercussions as determined by other
members of the project's leadership.

### Attribution

This Code of Conduct is adapted from the [Contributor Covenant][homepage], version 1.4,
available at [http://contributor-covenant.org/version/1/4][version]

[homepage]: http://contributor-covenant.org

[version]: http://contributor-covenant.org/version/1/4/

# Next.js Ecommerce site with WooCommerce backend

## Live URL: <https://next-woocommerce.dfweb.no>

## Table Of Contents (TOC)

- [Installation](#Installation)
- [WordPress GraphQL CORS](#wordpress-graphql-cors)
- [Features](#Features)
- [Design System](#design-system)
- [Lighthouse Performance Monitoring](#lighthouse-performance-monitoring)

- [Issues](#Issues)
- [Troubleshooting](#Troubleshooting)
- [TODO](#TODO)

## Installation

1.  Install and activate the following required plugins, in your WordPress plugin directory:

- [WooCommerce](https://wordpress.org/plugins/woocommerce) Ecommerce for WordPress.
- [WPGraphQL](https://wordpress.org/plugins/wp-graphql) Exposes GraphQL for WordPress.
- [GraphQL for eCommerce (WooGraphQL)](https://github.com/wp-graphql/wp-graphql-woocommerce) Adds WooCommerce functionality to a WPGraphQL schema.


Optional plugin:

- [headless-wordpress](https://github.com/w3bdesign/headless-wp) Disables the frontend so only the backend is accessible. (optional)

The current release has been tested and is confirmed working with the following versions:

- WordPress version 7.1.3
- WooCommerce version 11.2.1
- WP GraphQL version 2.24.0
- WooGraphQL (GraphQL for eCommerce) version 1.0.3

2.  For debugging and testing, install either:

    <https://addons.mozilla.org/en-US/firefox/addon/apollo-developer-tools/> (Firefox)

    <https://chrome.google.com/webstore/detail/apollo-client-developer-t/jdkknkkbebbapilgoeccciglkfbmbnfm> (Chrome)

3.  Make sure WooCommerce has some products already

4.  Clone or fork the repo and modify `.env.example` and rename it to `.env`

    Then set the environment variables accordingly in Vercel or your preferred hosting solution.

    See <https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables>

5.  Modify the values according to your setup

6.  Start the server with `pnpm run dev`

7.  Enable COD (Cash On Demand) payment method in WooCommerce

8.  Add a product to the cart

9.  Proceed to checkout (Gå til kasse)

10. Fill in your details and place the order

## WordPress GraphQL CORS

When the storefront and WordPress use different origins, the GraphQL endpoint
must explicitly allow the storefront origin and credentialed requests. A wildcard
allowed origin does not work with this application's cookie-based authentication.

This repository includes a small [WordPress must-use plugin](wordpress/mu-plugins/headless-graphql-cors.php)
for deployments that need this configuration. Install it on the WordPress server
and configure the allowed origins separately for each customer and environment;
the plugin does not hardcode deployment URLs or trust any origins by default.

After uploading the plugin, open **Settings → GraphQL CORS** in WordPress admin.

Enter one trusted frontend origin per line and save. No server configuration file
edits are required.

These are frontend origins, not the WordPress backend URL. Do not include paths,
trailing slashes, or wildcards. Add any other staging/preview origin explicitly
only when it should be trusted. An empty list blocks cross-origin storefront
requests and triggers an admin warning. Clear server/CDN caches after changing
the configuration, then reload the storefront.

See the [WordPress CORS setup guide](DOCS/wordpress-cors.md) for installation,
sample configuration, verification, and troubleshooting. No third-party CORS
plugin is required. If another plugin, web server, or CDN already manages CORS,
avoid overlapping or conflicting rules. Deploying the Next.js application alone
does not install the WordPress plugin.

## Features

- Next.js version 16.1.6
- React version 19.2.4
- Typescript
- Tests with Playwright
- Connect to Woocommerce GraphQL API and list name, price and display image for products
- Support for simple products and variable products
- Cart handling and checkout with WooCommerce using Zustand for state management
  - Persistent cart state with localStorage sync
  - Efficient updates through selective subscriptions
  - Type-safe cart operations
  - Cash On Delivery payment method
- Product search powered by WooCommerce (WPGraphQL), no external search service required
- Meets WCAG accessibility standards where possible
- Placeholder for products without images
- State Management:
  - Zustand for global state management
  - Apollo Client with GraphQL
- React Hook Form
- Native HTML5 form validation
- Animations with Framer motion, Styled components and Animate.css
- Loading spinner created with Styled Components
- Shows page load progress with Nprogress during navigation
- Fully responsive design
- Category and product listings
- Show stock status
- Pretty URLs with builtin Nextjs functionality
- Tailwind 3 for styling
- JSDoc comments
- Automated Lighthouse performance monitoring
  - Continuous performance, accessibility, and best practices checks
  - Automated reports on every pull request
  - Performance metrics tracking for:
    - Performance score
    - Accessibility compliance
    - Best practices adherence
    - SEO optimization
    - Progressive Web App readiness
- Product filtering:
  - Dynamic color filtering using Tailwind's color system
  - Mobile-optimized filter layout
  - Accessible form controls with ARIA labels
  - Price range slider
  - Size and color filters
  - Product type categorization
  - Sorting options (popularity, price, newest)

## Design System

This project uses a custom design system built with Tailwind CSS design tokens. The system is defined in [`tailwind.config.js`](tailwind.config.js) and [`globals.css`](src/styles/globals.css).

### Color Palette

The palette uses a muted slate-blue primary with warm neutral surfaces — chosen to feel calm, Nordic, and appropriate for a home goods store.

| Token           | Hex       | Usage                                           |
| --------------- | --------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
| `primary`       | `#3B6B8A` | Buttons, links, focus rings, active states      |
| `primary-light` | `#5A8BA8` | Hover accents, info states                      |
| `primary-dark`  | `#254D6B` | Mobile nav, button hover, dark accents          |
| `accent`        | `#4A8F8F` | Secondary accent (teal)                         |
| `surface`       | `#FAF9F7` | Page background, cards, inputs (warm off-white) |
| `surface-alt`   | `#F3F1ED` | Image placeholders, alternating surfaces        |
| `border`        | `#E5E2DC` | All borders and dividers                        |
| `text`          | `#2C2C2C` | Primary body text (warm near-black)             |
| `text-muted`    | `#6B6862` | Secondary text, descriptions                    |
| `text-light`    | `#9C9890` | Tertiary text, strikethrough prices             |
| `success`       | `#2D8A5E` | Stock status, success messages                  |
| `warning`       | `#C4882A` | Warning states                                  |
| `error`         | `#B83B2A` | Sale prices, errors, destructive actions        |

### Typography

- Uses Tailwind's default system font stack
- Type scale defined from `text-xs` (0.75rem) to `text-5xl` (3rem)
- Hierarchy rules: product card title `text-xl font-bold` > price `text-lg`, detail page h1 `text-2xl md:text-3xl font-light` > card title

### Accessibility

- Global `focus-visible` ring (`2px solid primary`) on all interactive elements
- `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` disables all animations and transitions
- Form inputs display inline error messages with `role="alert"` and `aria-invalid`
- Required fields marked with asterisks
- Proper `type` (`email`, `tel`) and `autocomplete` attributes on checkout inputs
- Descriptive `alt` text on images
- `disabled:cursor-not-allowed` on buttons
- Semantic HTML throughout (`<nav>`, `<main>`, `<section>`, `<label htmlFor>`)

### Button Variants

| Variant     | Appearance                | Use Case                                |
| ----------- | ------------------------- | --------------------------------------- |
| `primary`   | Solid blue (`bg-primary`) | Main CTAs — KJØP, GÅ TIL KASSE, BESTILL |
| `secondary` | Solid red (`bg-error`)    | Destructive actions — Fjern             |
| `hero`      | White on dark overlay     | Hero section CTA                        |
| `filter`    | Bordered, toggleable      | Size filter buttons                     |
| `reset`     | Light gray surface        | Reset filters                           |

All buttons include `hover`, `active:scale-[0.98]`, `focus-visible`, and `disabled` states with `duration-200` transitions.

### CSS Custom Properties

Design tokens are also available as CSS custom properties in [`globals.css`](src/styles/globals.css) for use outside Tailwind:

```css
var(--color-primary)      /* #3B6B8A */
var(--color-surface)      /* #FAF9F7 */
var(--color-text)         /* #2C2C2C */
var(--color-error)        /* #B83B2A */
```

## Lighthouse Performance Monitoring

This project uses automated Lighthouse testing through GitHub Actions to ensure high-quality web performance. On every pull request:

- Performance, accessibility, best practices, and SEO are automatically evaluated
- Results are posted directly to the pull request
- Minimum score thresholds are enforced for:
  - Performance: Analyzing loading performance, interactivity, and visual stability
  - Accessibility: Ensuring WCAG compliance and inclusive design
  - Best Practices: Validating modern web development standards
  - SEO: Checking search engine optimization fundamentals
  - PWA: Assessing Progressive Web App capabilities

View the latest Lighthouse results in the GitHub Actions tab under the "Lighthouse Check" workflow.

## Accessibility Testing (pa11y)

In addition to Lighthouse, this project runs dedicated accessibility testing with [pa11y](https://github.com/pa11y/pa11y) through GitHub Actions (the "A11y" workflow). On every push and pull request to the `main`/`master` branch:

- The app is built and started locally, then scanned with pa11y against its main public pages:
  - `/` (home)
  - `/produkter` (products)
  - `/kategorier` (categories)
  - `/handlekurv` (cart)
  - `/kasse` (checkout)
  - `/logg-inn` (login)
- Reports for each page are uploaded as workflow artifacts (`pa11y-reports`)
- The job fails if any accessibility errors are detected

Note: Dynamic product and category pages (`/produkt/[slug]`, `/kategori/[slug]`) are not scanned in CI because they depend on a live WooCommerce GraphQL backend that may be unreachable during the workflow. Accessibility issues are almost always template-level, so scanning the shared pages above provides equivalent coverage. The authenticated account page (`/min-konto`) is also excluded since it requires login.

View the latest accessibility results in the GitHub Actions tab under the "A11y" workflow.

## Security

This project runs automated OWASP security scanning on every push and pull request via GitHub Actions:

| Scan                    | Tool                                             | What It Catches                                       |
| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------- |
| 🔑 **Secret Detection** | [gitleaks](https://github.com/gitleaks/gitleaks) | Hardcoded API keys, passwords, tokens, private keys   |
| 🔍 **SAST**             | [Semgrep](https://semgrep.dev)                   | OWASP Top 10 + React/Next.js-specific vulnerabilities |
| 📦 **Dependencies**     | [Trivy](https://github.com/aquasecurity/trivy)   | Known CVEs in npm packages (CRITICAL/HIGH)            |

Custom Semgrep rules cover OWASP categories A01–A10 including XSS prevention, injection detection, authentication checks, SSRF protection, and security misconfiguration. View results in the GitHub Actions tab under the "OWASP Security Scan" workflow.

## Troubleshooting

### Cross-origin GraphQL requests are blocked

Follow the [WordPress CORS setup guide](DOCS/wordpress-cors.md). Configure CORS on
the GraphQL server, not through frontend response headers, and keep cookie
credentials enabled for authentication.

### I am getting a cart undefined error or other GraphQL errors

Check that you are using the latest version of the [wp-graphql-woocommerce](https://github.com/wp-graphql/wp-graphql-woocommerce) plugin

### The products page isn't loading

Check the attributes of the products. Right now the application requires Size and Color.

## Issues

Overall the application is working as intended, but it has not been tested extensively in a production environment.
More testing and debugging is required before deploying it in a production environment.

With that said, keep the following in mind:

- Currently only simple products and variable products work without any issues. Other product types are not known to work.
- Only Cash On Delivery (COD) is currently supported. More payment methods may be added later.

This project is tested with BrowserStack.

## TODO

- Implement UserRegistration.component.tsx in a registration page
- Add user dashboard with order history
- Add Cloudflare Turnstile on registration page
- Ensure email is real on registration page
- Add total to cart/checkout page
- Copy billing address to shipping address
- Hide products not in stock

        sm: '0 1px 2px 0 rgba(44, 44, 44, 0.05)',

        lg: '0 10px 15px -3px rgba(44, 44, 44, 0.08), 0 4px 6px -4px rgba(44, 44, 44, 0.05)',
      },
    },
  },
  plugins: [],
};
export default config;
